Battery Manager 4 Installation

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                              Page 6 of 363
1 Battery Manager 4 Introduction

Battery Manager ­ the all-in-one software solution for battery testing and formation. Process
efficiency through integration.

Battery Manager ...

    · integrates all of the powerful features found in existing testing and formation systems such
         as "BTS-600" (battery testing), "Formation Supervisor III" (Battery formation) and "PLT"
         (production line testing).

    · connects all Digatron / Firing Circuits devices as well as external devices such as
         temperature chambers or scales to one common database.

    · reduces your in-process inventory using a software algorithm to optimize the utilization of
         test and formation process resources.

    · provides an easy to use operator interface where the job number is the key to all related
         data generated either in the laboratory or formation processes. All relevant job steps are
         generated and displayed clearly for each battery in test or formation.

    · operates with Windows and SQL server. The customer's Windows network can be used to
         operate Battery Manager 4 (BM4) from any workstation within the network. Remote control
         of all equipment connected to the network is also possible via Internet access.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 7 of 363
1. System Diagram

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 8 of 363
1.1. To start Battery Manager

Prior to starting Battery Manager, the servers have to be activated. Then the actual Battery
Manager program can be started on the Workstation. Depending on the system's configuration
(number of PCs, number and location of Communication Servers and Workstations) the start
sequence may vary. The Battery Manager system will provide a service to deal with this
automatically.

1.2. Establishing the Data Communication

Battery Manager consists basically of four software components:

· BM Workstation
· BM ServerManager
· BM Communication Server
· BM Database

BM Workstation
The Workstation is the actual user interface for controlling circuits and graphical evaluation of
registration data. The battery-related program maintenance and other functions are started here,
too.

BM Communication Server
The Communication Server is an independent application that works as communication interface
towards the physical circuits. In accordance with various interfaces the Communication Server
transports data to and from the respective circuit. One system can have several Communication
Servers. Each server provides one Partyline. The number of circuits on a Partyline depends on the
existing installation.

BM ServerManager
The ServerManager coordinates and prepares the data between the Communication Servers and
the database.

           The ServerManager port (7001) must be enabled for access to remote workstations in an
           activated Windows firewall.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 9 of 363
1.3. Database Architecture

Battery Manager requires a database and is therefore linked to Microsoft SQL Server. This manual
does not describe the handling of databases in detail. It is assumed that the BM system
administrator knows the basics of operating and maintaining the databases.

Circuit Database
The Circuit database contains tables that include information about:

· all available circuits (physically available circuits)
· Communication Server information
· program information
· batteries
· the name of the session

User Database
The User database stores all information about the Users and groups of users (departments). This
database is always password protected. The password is shipped with the initial system as part of
the license agreement.

1.4. Database Maintenance

Some basic database maintenance is required for the BM system to run at optimum performance.
While adding and deleting permanent records to the database, the database performance will
decrease over time. To compact the used databases periodically all connections to the database
should be disconnected and then the database should be compacted.

If data registration is used to store all registration data that occurred during the program, the
database will grow quickly. To maintain a reasonably sized database, older data should be deleted
periodically.

To back up the system, regular database backups are necessary. These backups then contain all
information needed to restore the system in case of e.g. a Hard Drive crash. A weekly backup is a
recommended method of long term data archiving. Battery Manager therefore delivers several
automatic scripts that will be installed if required.

You can find detailed information about maintenance of your BM4 database in the appendix,
chapter Battery Manager 4 Installation ­ After the Installation.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 10 of 363
1.5. Login

You need to log in for many of Battery Manager's functions. Press F5 or choose Log In under the
Control item on the main menu to invoke the login dialog. Enter user name and corresponding
password in the places provided. Press OK.

When first logging in, these standard entries are available:
User name: Digatron
Password: bm4
User rights are defined with the Password Maintenance dialog (chapter 7).
Actions like login and logout are displayed as events in the workstation window at the bottom of the
screen.

The Workstation window also shows e.g. starts, stops and interruptions of circuits, adding and
deleting batteries and changing or deleting programs.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 11 of 363
1.6. Logout

Press F4 or choose Log Out under the Control item on the main menu to log out. Prior user
access is immediately lost.

User rights are defined with the Password Maintenance dialog (chapter 7).

1.7. Event View

The Event Viewer in the lower area of Battery Manager window can be displayed using the Events
button in the quick start bar.

The Event Viewer consists of various tabs in which the system events are displayed depending on
their origin. The Event Viewer shows all events of the system that have been in the past, as well as
events from other PCs / workstations that are connected to this Battery Manager system.

By default, the Workstation and ComServerstatus tabs are displayed. Four additional tabs can
be shown and hidden. You can do this by right-clicking on one of the two tabs.

Instead of right-clicking on a tab, the additional tabs can also be shown or hidden using the plus on
the far left:

Then the menu also appears:

The maintenance tab contains information on backup, archive and deletion of data and
databases.

The Service tab shows messages from the BM Control Service.

Each tab has an icon, which is normally green. If new events are loaded in a tab, the
corresponding icon changes to yellow for a short time. If the newly loaded events contain error
messages, this is indicated by a red icon. The icon remains red until this error has been viewed.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 12 of 363
In addition, error messages in the tab are marked by the fact that the complete line is highlighted in
red. Warnings are displayed in orange.

The entries in the individual tabs can still be sorted, filtered and copied.
The AutoScroll option (in the right-click menu) inserts new events at the top or bottom and scrolls
to the new events accordingly.

By activating the option Newest top, new entries are inserted at the top of the list. If Newest top is
deactivated, new entries are added at the bottom of the list.
By right-clicking, depending on the column in which you right-click, you can set different display
options, for example a filter based on the period of the saved events.

By clicking on e.g. only the events of the last 60 minutes are displayed for 1 hour.
The set time interval is shown in square brackets in the header:

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                                     Page 13 of 363
After right-clicking in the Event Viewer and selecting Number in the menu that appears, the
maximum number of entries in the tab can be selected. A maximum of 1000 entries are possible:

In the Workstation tab, the entries can also be filtered based on their origin. This can be
determined by right-clicking on the Origin column and then clicking on Filter in the menu that
appears. Now you can choose from the displayed filter elements, whose events are to be
displayed.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 14 of 363
By right-clicking in the Text column of the Server tab and clicking on Filter in the menu that
appears, it can be determined from which systems (e.g. PLT) events are displayed:

If you click on a text in the Text column with the right mouse button, you can enter a character
string for filtering after clicking on Filter in the context menu that appears under Filter text:

After clicking OK, only those entries are displayed that contain the previously entered character
string in the Text column.

In the tab ComServer status existing ComServer connections are marked with a green
checkmark. This means that the connection is ok since the last update.
If a ComSever is marked with an orange exclamation mark, there is a warning that the last
successful update was more than 2 minutes ago.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 15 of 363
It is possible to copy events and messages to the clipboard. This is done by selecting individual
lines while holding down the Ctrl key and then Ctrl + C or RMT and selecting Copy from the
context menu that appears. Marked lines can be placed on the clipboard. You can also use
Ctrl + A to mark all the entries in a tab and then copy them to the clipboard. The content thus
copied can then be copied from the clipboard for analysis, e.g. copied into a text file.

When the BM4 application is closed, the settings made are automatically saved. The next time the
BM4 application is started, the settings will be available again.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 16 of 363
2 Floor Display

The Floor Display gives the user a broad view of a large number of circuits. The Floor Display
consists of multiple color blocks and, optionally, graphic elements. Each block represents a given
circuit. The circuit name is in the upper left corner of the block. The color of the block represents
the status of the circuit. This allows the user to determine the status of many circuits with a quick
scan.

Various graphic elements, including walls, doors, boxes and windows, can be added to the Floor
Display layout to enhance realism. Text blocks can also be added to provide orientation
information. The user determines the size of the blocks, their positions, and adds structural
elements during the Floor Display design process.

To open the Floor View, click View|Floor or click the Floor entry from the Shortcut Bar. Multiple
Floor views can be opened at the same time.

Moving within the Floor Display

The Floor Display shows one portion of the entire floor. The user can change the viewed area by
using the page-up and page-down keys or the scroll bars. This will alter the currently displayed set
of circuits.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 17 of 363
2.1 Zooming In and Out

The Floor Display can be zoomed to adjust the number of displayed circuits. There are several
ways to zoom:
Use the Floor Display Zoom/Pan toolbar:

         Sets the cursor to normal point and click mode.
         Each click of the right mouse key zooms out and increases the number of circuits
         displayed. Each click of the left mouse button zooms in and decreases the number of
         circuits displayed. Pressing the left mouse key and dragging the pointer allows you to box
         the circuits that you want to zoom in and decreases the number of circuits displayed.

         Zooms out so that all circuits are displayed.
         Sets the magnification to 100%.

         Zooms into the selected items.

         Allows you to grip the display and drag it up/down and left/right.

Use the View Floor View functions:
· Zoom ­ Enables cursor/mouse base zoom (left/right mouse key).
· Zoom Normal ­ Sets the magnification to 100%.
· Zoom Percent ­ Provides pre-selected magnifications of 50% and 75% (zoom out), 100%

      (zoom normal) and 200% (zoom in).
· Zoom Custom ­ Allows you to specify the magnification percent.

· Zoom to Fit ­ Zooms out so that all circuits are displayed.
· Zoom to Selection ­ Zooms the selected element(s).

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                              Page 18 of 363
2.2 Adjust Toolbars

If a toolbar is not visible you can activate it by selecting the entry Toolbars from the View menu:

Click an entry from the list to make the corresponding toolbar visible. You can also hide toolbars by
deactivating entries.
The option Show Tooltips determines if a short description appears in a yellow popup window on
a screen when you point on a toolbar button.
The deactivation of the option Cool Look makes the toolbars appear more old-fashioned.
The option Large Buttons allows to enlarge the size of all toolbar buttons for better differentiation.
Use the New button for creating your own toolbars. Self-created toolbars can be deleted by
highlighting and then clicking the Delete button.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 19 of 363
The button Customize makes it possible to pick toolbar buttons from the Commands tab to your
self-created toolbar via drag and drop:

When clicking a symbol here you get a small description.
When the dialog Customize is open you can remove symbols from all toolbars by pulling them
away with the left mouse button pressed. The button Reset resets all toolbars to their initial state.

2.3 QuickView and QuattroView

Use QuickView
Placing the mouse pointer over a circuit box will activate the QuickView status display. Limited
status information, such as the circuit name, circuit status, battery type, etc. will be displayed in a
small window. This information will be automatically updated.

Move the mouse pointer away from a circuit box and the QuickView window will disappear.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 20 of 363
Disabling QuickView
The QuickView feature can be disabled if desired. When in Floor View, click menu entry View -
Properties and uncheck the QuickView option to disable.
Select Channels for QuickView
In Floor Designer choose QuickView Configuration from menu Edit to adjust QuickView to your
needs.

Dialog QuickView Configuration is identical with dialog Floor Configuration from chapter
3.7 Choosing Channels for Display.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 21 of 363
Use QuattroView

When double-clicking a circuit box you get an overview with detailed circuit information. This
requires that data is available for this circuit.

Top left you see the QuickView for the circuit.

Top right you see a graphical display. It shows the current by default. Other values can be chosen
as described in chapter 5.6 Graphical Display. Changed settings are stored for later QuattroViews
with the same program.

Bottom left you see the program associated with this circuit.

Bottom right you see detailed registration data for this circuit.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 22 of 363
2.4 Controlling Circuits

It is possible to control one or more circuits from the Floor Display.

Select the circuits that you wish to control:

· to select one circuit, click on it with the left mouse button
· to add to the selection set, hold down the Ctrl key and click on the additional circuits with the

      left mouse button
· to select multiple circuits that are near each other on the floor display layout, press the left

      mouse key and drag the pointer to outline the circuits that you want to control
· to select all circuits, press Ctrl+A
· you can deselect a selected circuit by clicking on it with keeping the shift key pressed

Control the circuits in one of the following ways:

· position the mouse pointer inside one of the selected circuits, click the right mouse button and
      select the desired control function from the menu

· by using the associated control function key, such as F9 to start
· select the control function on the Control toolbar or from the menu Control

Elements on the toolbar Control:

         Start

         Stop

         Continue

         Interrupt

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 23 of 363
2.5 Start Circuits

To start a circuit, highlight it in the Floor View and then click Start from the Control menu or click
the symbol from the Control toolbar. In the status view you may as well right click an entry in
the column Name and select the desired function from the appearing menu. You can as well select
one or several circuits by dragging a frame around them followed by pressing F9.

When selecting Start there appears a dialog asking for various start parameters.

This dialog might look different depending on your hardware and software configuration.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 24 of 363
Perform the following steps here:
Selected Circuit(s)
Choose a circuit from the list on the left.

Selection
Select Battery Type, Battery or Program / Schedule from field Selection.

When activating option Battery Type, you are able to make a selection in selection box Battery
Type below. The selection boxes Battery and Program then only show batteries and programs
that fit this battery type.

When selecting option Battery, the selection box Program only lists programs that have been
assigned with this battery in battery dialog. If no program is assigned to a selected battery, no
programs are shown.

When selecting option Program / Schedule selection box Program (below) shows all existing
programs.

Selection box Battery Type allows defining the type of battery. This is only possible if option
Battery Type has been chosen above. Otherwise selection box Battery Type is not active and
has no effect.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                         Page 25 of 363
Use selection box Battery to select a battery of the type you just chose. The list under Battery
only shows batteries belonging to the selected battery type.

Use field Program / Schedule to select a program for the battery you just chose. Depending on
your system settings you can also enter either a program name or number directly and confirm with
the arrow down  key.
From the following possibilities some can be deactivated in file BM.ini (to be found by default
under C:\Digatron\Battery Manager\WKS) in section [CONFIG_CIRCSTART].
The Filter button opens a dialog of the Program Manager, with which you can find individual
programs more quickly in the program management using the filter function.
Deferred Start
When starting a circuit, a deferred start is possible.

Here you can determine date and time of a circuit that is about to be started. If you want to run a
circuit immediately, leave these fields unchanged or empty.

Comment (BM.ini entry: Comments=TRUE)
Enter a comment. It will be displayed in the registration data overview.

Order No (BM.ini entry: OrderNo=TRUE)
If you insert an order number, you can use it in the naming scheme as described below. It will be
displayed in the registration data overview also.

Nominal Capacity (BM.ini entry: UseCNom=TRUE)
Enter the nominal capacity of the battery. For BTS-600 this definition can only be done in the
battery dialog, see chapter 8 Battery Maintenance.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 26 of 363
The nominal capacity is required for using CNom (as limit or registration) and ACN or ACNX (as
nominal value, limit or registration) in programs. ACN represents a current depending on the
nominal capacity. ACNX represents a current depending on the nominal capacity and the time X
hours and is not available for all systems.

The following example shows the usage of ACN, ACNX and CNom:

Step 2: If the nominal capacity is 1 Ah, the battery is charged with 1 × 1 Ah / 5 h = 0.2 A.
           A registration occurs every 0.2 x 1 Ah / 5 h = 0.04 A.

Step 3: This step is terminated when the current goes below 1 × 1 Ah / 5 h = 0.2 A.
Step 4: This step is terminated when 80% of the nominal capacity is reached.

           A registration occurs every 0.1 x 1 Ah = 0.1 Ah.

Number of Cells (BM.ini entry: UseCell=TRUE)
Enter the number of cells for the battery. The number of cells is required when using VNC (as a
nominal value or limit) in programs. For BTS-600 this definition can only be done in the battery
dialog, see chapter 8 Battery Maintenance.

The following example shows the usage of VNC:

If the battery has 6 cells, the step is terminated as soon as the voltage reaches 2.48 × 6 = 14.88 V.

Step (BM.ini entry: StartFromStep=TRUE)
Enter step number of program / formation schedule on which the circuit is to start.

Click button Start to start the selected circuit(s) or click Cancel to abort. When a circuit has started
you can recognize the new state of the circuit by its new color in the Circuit Overview.
After starting you can right-click the circuit and choose entry Show current Program from the
appearing menu to display the running program. The current program step is indicated by color.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 27 of 363
2.5.1 Special Circuit Start Parameters

100% Current
Enter 100% current. Percentage operators within the program / formation schedule will consider
this value prior to being sent to the circuit. For example, with 10 amps entered as 100% current, a
charge step with 40% is replaced with 4 A.

Circuits (A-E)
Select circuits to operate with a single program / formation schedule. Typically used with 2, 4 and 5
circuit MPG controllers.

Enable Registration (BM.ini entry: ShowRegistrationTemperatureEnable=TRUE)
Define if measuring data should be saved.

Enable Temperature Setpoints
Define if temperature setpoints should be calculated when running programs / formation schedules
contain percentage reductions.

Step Number (BM.ini entry: StepCycle=TRUE)
Enter the number of cycles for the program.

Session Naming Scheme (BM.ini entry: SessionNaming=TRUE)

You can use the field Session Naming Scheme to define a name for the current procedure. The
session can later be found under this name in the database. You can either define the name as a
whole or use placeholders, so-called tokens, that will be filled with dynamic data. Tokens are
embedded inside the `<' and `>' signs. As an example, <date> is a token and the system fills that
token at start time with the actual date. All characters that are not recognized as tokens are copied
into the session name as entered.

Following the list of valid tokens:

<program> Inserts the program name.

<battery> Inserts the selected battery type.

<location> Inserts the actual circuit ID.

<operator> Inserts the operator name logged in during the formation start.

<date>Inserts the date like e.g. 10/31/06 (includes the forward slash).

<date1>  Inserts the date like e.g. 103106 (no slash included).

<orderno> Inserts the order number entered into the field in this dialog.

Battery Manager User Manual                   99.3                          Page 28 of 363
<inc>   Counter increments by 1 every time it's used. Increments up to 99999 before it
        wraps around. No leading zeros are displayed.

<inc1>  Counter increments by 1 every time it's used. No leading zeros. Counter range 0-9.

<inc2>  Counter increments by 1 every time it's used. 1 leading zero. Counter range 01-99.

<inc3>  Counter increments by 1 every time it's used. 2 leading zeros.
        Counter range 001-999.

<inc4>  Counter increments by 1 every time it's used. 3 leading zeros.
        Counter range 0001-9999.

<inc5>  Counter increments by 1 every time it's used. 4 leading zeros.
        Counter range 00001-99999.

You can see a preview of the result of your definitions in the Sample field.

Some examples of session names:

Session Naming Scheme               Result (Example)

Formation-<inc3>-<battery>          Formation-002-Pb0123
Formation-<inc5>-<battery>          Formation-00002-Pb0123
<program><date><location>           PbFastCharge10/09/00033A
<program>, <date>, <location>       PbFastCharge, 10/09/00, 033A
Test:<program>, <date>, <location>  Test:PbFastCharge, 10/09/00, 033A

A created Session Naming Scheme is saved when the option Save new scheme on exit is
activated. The saved scheme appears in the list box under Session Naming Scheme the next
time the start dialog is opened.

If you enter an Order No. you can use it in the naming scheme like described above. It will be
displayed in the registration data overview as well.

2.9 Reset Circuits

To reset circuits, you need to be logged in as Supervisor. Select the circuit you want to reset. To
select several circuits, keep the Ctrl key pressed while clicking on circuits in Floor or Status View.

Right click a highlighted circuit in the Floor View and then click Reset circuit(s) from the appearing
menu. You may as well right click an entry in the column Name of the Status view and select the
entry Reset circuit(s).

An overview with the circuits you selected appears.

Circuits can be reset in two different ways. Observe the notes in the upper part of the window.

The simple reset only cleans up the entry in the Dispo. To do this, click on the Reset button. This
means that BM4 cleans up its circuit status information independently of the control software in the
HW controller.

If you also check the box "Circuit hard reset", the ComServer and the corresponding task in the
controller are also reset. The selected circles end in STOP.

Please note the hints in the upper part of this window. You may then click the button Reset to
confirm your selection.

You can also reset circuits from the dispo list, see chapter 10 for details.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 33 of 363
2.10 Circuit Status and Circuit Messages

Every circuit of Battery Manager has a defined status. This status is represented by the
color of the circuit in Floor Display.

Default colors are:

Circuits that are offline are displayed in  .

Circuits in STOP mode are displayed in dark gray.

Circuits in Interrupt mode are displayed in light gray.

Circuits in Pause mode are displayed in green color.

Circuits in Charge mode are displayed in       color.

Circuits in Recharge mode are displayed in light yellow color.

Circuits in Discharge mode are displayed in blue color.

Circuits for which a Deferred Start is programmed are displayed in light blue.

Circuits in Error mode are displayed in red color.

Circuits in black color (BTS-600 only) indicate a compiler error (Generator).

Colors are also displayed in column Operator of Status View (chapter 6).

There are different symbols that can appear behind an operator or instead of an operator in the
overview or in QuickView (chapter 2.3) or in column Operator of the registration data (chapter 5)
or in the status view (chapter 6):

Symbol   behind        only not for                                      Meaning
        operator     BTS-600 BTS-600
    #                                                        Load Management (LOM)
  ???                                                              unknown operator
                         
     !                                                associated registration was not saved
   ...                                                       deferred start / initialization
   ...                                                              data is collected
    *                                                               error or interrupt
    **                                                               buffer overflow
   ***                                                              data is collected

A buffer overflow (indicated by **) leads to data loss. To prevent buffer overflows you
can take several measures, depending on your system: reduce the number of
registrations in the associated program or reduce the number of running circuits.

Battery Manager User Manual                    99.3             Page 34 of 363
For BTS-600 circuits three special states can be shown instead of an operator: RESET, BOOT and
ZERO.

 State                                                           Meaning
RESET                        The circuit has been reset but has not yet been restarted.
BOOT                         T1he circuit is currently restarted.
ZERO                         After a restart the circuit is not yet ready.

For BTS-600 circuits the status column Result shows error messages:

     Text in column Result                                       Meaning
Registration drop            A registration is missing.
Start failed                 Starting a program failed.
Program file not found       A program could not be found.
Circuit died                 The communication with a circuit terminated unexpectedly.
Dispo error entry != 0       The entry "Error" in the Dispo is or was unequal 0.
Program download cancelled   The download of a program was cancelled.
Generator found errors       An unspecified error occurred.
Generator program not found  A program could not be found.

2.11 Saving and Recalling the Current Floor Display Configuration

The current Floor Display configuration can be saved for future reuse. If you have disabled
QuickView, for example, then you can save this configuration and recall it next time it is needed.
The configuration is saved to a file.

To save the configuration:

· Click File|Save As
· Be sure that Save in: says data
· Enter a file name. The file will automatically be saved with the required .flr extension.

To recall the configuration and start Floor Display:

· Click File|Open
· Be sure that Look in: says data
· Use the Files of type: drop down list to select .flr
· Select a file name from the list.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                    Page 35 of 363
2.12 Error Types

The reason for certain errors messages (e.g. ER5) depends on the installed circuit software. Here
are the most common error types and their reasons:

Error 1 (ER1) indicates that a program has been started at a circuit and there is no voltage at the
power unit.

Error 2 (ER2) indicates that during the program operation the measured voltage is by 5 % higher
than the maximum voltage value of the power unit.

Error 3 (ER3) indicates that the measured current differs by more than +5 % of the final value
from the nominal current value entered in the program.

Error 4 (ER4) indicates that the measured current differs by more than -5 % of the final value
from the nominal current value entered in the program.

Error 5 (ER5) indicates that due to a mechanical fault, one of the contactors (relays) is on an
inadmissible position. Other possible reason: plausibility error.

Error 6 (ER6) indicates that configuration data from controller could not be read.

Error 7 (ER7) indicates that an error occurred on the CAN bus.

Error 8 (ER8) indicates that connection to CANXION has broken off.

Error 9 (ER9) indicates that the global voltage limit has been reached.

Error 10 (ER10) indicates that OPC reports an error.

 3.2 Importing and Deleting Circuits

Importing circuits
You have the possibility to import circuits that already exist in the database. To achieve this, click
menu Edit ­ Import circuits from database. A dialog appears:

This dialog lists all circuits that are stored in the database but currently not included in the floor.
Under Floor Layout at the right of the dialog you can define how circuits are arranged in the floor.
The Preview indicates how circuits are arranged.

After clicking button Configure Displayed Channels you can select which values are displayed in
circuit boxes of Floor. For more information see chapter 3.7 Choosing Channels for Display. When
importing, channels that were displayed during the last import of this circuit type are now again
displayed by default.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                                    Page 43 of 363
With a long list of circuits in dialog Import Circuit Controllers from Database it might not be easy
to find a certain controller. You may therefore enter a string under Filter Controllers so that only
controllers that include this string are displayed in the list. Enter a string that displayed controllers
should have in common. Regard upper and lower case and do not use
replacement characters like *.
Selection Box Filter Channel Types can be used to only display circuits of a certain channel type.
When using button Apply currently set filters are employed on the list of controllers. You may
define several filters one after the other and activate them with button Apply. New filters are added
to existing filters.

Use button Clear to delete all active filters. The list now shows all controllers again.

Click a circuit from the list to add it to the floor or keep key Ctrl pressed while clicking to add
several circuits at once. To select all circuits, press Ctrl+A. Click Import to confirm.

Remove circuit symbols

You have the possibility to delete circuit symbols from the floor without deleting circuits from the
database. To achieve this, click menu Edit ­ Delete circuit symbols. A dialog appears:

This dialog lists all circuits that are currently included in the floor. Circuits previously selected in
Floor Designer are pre-selected in the list. Click a circuit to delete it from the floor or keep key Ctrl
pressed while clicking to remove several circuits at once. Click Remove to confirm.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 44 of 363
Remove circuits from system
You have the possibility to remove circuits from the system completely. Select circuits you want to
delete with the mouse. Then click menu entry Edit ­ Remove circuits from system. A security
question appears asking if you really want to delete the selected circuits. After confirmation with
Yes circuits are removed from the system.

 3.3 Moving and Resizing Circuits

To move a circuit box:

· Move the mouse pointer over the box until a four-way arrow appears.
· Hold down the left mouse button and move the box. The box will snap to the grid allowing easy

      alignment of the boxes.

To resize a circuit box:

· Move the mouse pointer to the desired circuit and click the left mouse button. Grab handles
      will appear.

· Move the mouse pointer over the desired grab handle until an arrow appears.
· Hold down the left mouse button and resize the box. The box will snap to the grid allowing

      easy alignment of the boxes.

Moving and dimensioning circuit boxes can be undone step by step with menu entry Edit ­ Undo
or using shortcut Ctrl+Z.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 45 of 363
3.4 Adjusting Floor Size

The size of the floor display layout can be modified to fit the desired number of circuit boxes.
Ideally, this size should be only slightly larger than necessary to fully contain all circuit boxes.
Remember, the floor display layout will typically be larger than what can be viewed with the floor
display view, and you will have to scroll to the desired area or zoom out to fit more of the floor
display layout into the floor display view.

The size of the floor display layout is in inches. Note that the Undo/Redo feature is not available
when adjusting the floor size.

· Click the entry Measurements and Size from the Edit menu. The dialog box Measurements
      and Size appears with the tab Size and Units.

· Be sure that Unit of Measure says Inches or Centimeters.
· Set the Canvas Width and Canvas Height as necessary. Experiment with these numbers.

The tab Drawing Scale enables you to determine the scale of the display on the screen (e.g. when
drawing rooms).

The tab Coordinate Mapping defines the mapping mode and the range of values to display.

 3.5 Adding Graphic Elements

You can create a true bird's eye view of the floor by adding graphic elements to the Floor Display
Layout. Walls, doorways, windows, support beams, computer rooms, and signs can be added
where needed. In addition, text boxes can be added for orientation purposes, such as North West
Entrance, etc.

Add building elements by using the Building toolbar:

· Click on any of the building structure icons. A shapes icon will be added to the mouse pointer.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 46 of 363
· Move the mouse pointer to the desired spot and click the left mouse button. The building
      structure will be inserted.

· These elements can be used to construct walls and insert doors and windows. Stretch the wall
      element to connect two 90° corner elements.

Add a text box by using the Draw toolbar:

· Click on the text icon and position and insert a text box.
· Change the text box size by selecting it and using the grab handles to stretch it.
· Change the text and font as necessary. Move the mouse pointer to the text block, click the

      right mouse button and select Properties. The Component Properties dialog box will appear
      opened to the General tab.
· To modify the text, select the Text tab. Click the option box Multiple Lines to create a
      multiline text. With the option Word Break you can create a wordwrap.
· To modify the font, select the Font tab.
· To determine the graphical styling, select the Fill tab.
· To change the border around the box, select the Line tab.
· To change the properties of a text box, select the Edit tab.

You may as well click an element once and then select Properties from the Edit menu to see the
Component Properties dialog.

Add a box by using the Draw toolbar:

· Click on the right (white) box icon and position and insert a box.
· Change the text box size by selecting it and using the grab handles to stretch it.

Additional capabilities:

· From the menu Drawing you can then select from a number of graphic elements, including
      line, rectangle, closed curve, ellipse, etc.

· From the menu you can select Align, Nudge and Rotate to change the position and
      orientation of a graphic element.

· From the Align menu you may allocate chosen elements a common position. Select elements
      by clicking them one after the other while keeping the shift key pressed. Pick the point of
      reference from the Align menu. When selecting e.g. Align Top, selected elements will be
      assigned the same vertical position considering their top edge. The point of reference is
      always determined by the element you selected last.

· The Size menu gives chosen elements identical dimensions or equal distances. Select
      elements by clicking them one after the other while keeping the shift key pressed. Pick the
      dimension you want to match. (Same Width/Same Height/Same Size) Reference size is
      always defined by the element you selected last.
     Entries Space across and Space down from the Size menu will arrange equal horizontal or
     vertical distances between a minimum number of three elements you selected like described
     before.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 47 of 363
Stacking graphic elements and adjusting the stacking order:

· You can stack graphic elements on top of each other. For example, you could create an ellipse
      and then place a text box on top of it. In this case, it may be necessary to adjust the order of
      the items as they appear, e.g., you want the text box on top of the ellipse.

· To adjust the order, move the mouse pointer to the stack, click the right mouse button and
      select Order.... Use Bring to Front, Send to Back, Bring Forward and Send Backward as
      necessary. Experiment for best results. Note that the Undo/Redo feature is not available
      when ordering graphic elements.

To use a changed or newly created Floor Design as new standard Floor View, store the layout
under the name FloorDesign.fld into the data folder. When calling the Floor View for the next
time, this layout will be used as a template.

3.8 Subfloor setup

To keep an overview of a large number of circuits, it can be useful to create subfloors with
containers. This allows you to start and stop several circuits at the same time. If you open a
container, further details about the systems or system groups are displayed.

           To set up the subfloor, you must be logged into BM4 as a supervisor

In preparation, a ComServer must be created. This can also be a "dummy", which then only serves
internal purposes. Alternatively, an existing ComServer can be used, in which case the control of
the circuits is also possible (see example below).

A container is to be created with the Floor Designer Wizard. Select a ComServer under Device
server.

           There may be containers that are not assigned to an active ComServer, but to a dummy
           ComServer.

The color tables 501 - 503 can be used as color table for the container. Alternatively, you can
create your own color table from the range 2000-2999.
In the following example the customer specific color table 502 has been used.

           The settings for current, voltage, device name, extension table and default channels are
           not used and are arbitrary.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                                   Page 50 of 363
Under circuit name a name prefix can be set. With entries in start address and Number, the start
and number of containers can be specified.
With Number of columns/rows and the setting Sort order a reasonable arrangement of the
containers can be predefined in case of multiple containers.

In the following example, the container "Tower 11" is a link into the subfloor. The subfloor is a floor
file with the name of the container, in the above example: "Tower11.Flr". This file contains the
contiguous circuits of the tower.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 51 of 363
Double-click on the created container to open the dialog for the circuit properties. Here you have to
select the circuit/channel type (Container_XY_AB) and enter the color table 502 (if this has not
already been done when creating the container), see next picture.

After that, the circuits can be drawn into the container. However, this is only for visual purposes
and has no influence on the operations themselves.
These circuits in the container can be controlled individually, but at the same time only if all have
been selected or the container has been selected individually.

           Attention! The file names of the subfloor and the container name must match so that the
           respective subfloor is opened by double-clicking.
The created floordesign is to be saved in the folder C:\Digatron\Battery Manager\WKS\data. The
file has the extension FLD.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 52 of 363
The extensions of the files must be renamed to the extension FLR for each container. Any
warnings from Windows Explorer must be answered accordingly.

In the database, the container is linked to the ComServer and created as a "circuit" in the
"tblCircuit" table. If a real existing ComServer is used instead of a dummy ComServer, ALL circuits
of this ComServer can be controlled via the Start/Stop/Interrupt/Continue operations.

Edit the table LocationStatus
Open the BM4 database with "SQL Server Management Studio".
Find the table "LocationStatus", right click and select "Edit top x rows".

To get some color for the container in the floor, the value in the column "StepIndex" in the table
dbo.LocationStatus must be set to "0" or a value according to the color table. The operator can be
changed to the value "idle".

           Only the ,,StepIndex" column is relevant for the color section in the floor. All other columns
           do not have to be changed!

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 53 of 363
Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 54 of 363
4 Workspace Manager

The Workspace is the appearance or layout of Battery Manager as seen on the screen. This layout
consists of the position and size of the Battery Manager application itself, status and floor windows
and the position, size and docking status of each dockable toolbar.

The current workspace is saved on system exit under the name ..\data\STA-[Computer name]-
UNI.wsp. This workspace will automatically be restored during system restart.

4.1 Saving and recalling the Workspace

It is possible to save the current workspace to a different file name for future use.

To save the workspace:

· Click File|Save Workspace As
· Be sure that Save in: says C:\Digatron\Battery Manager\WKS\data
· Enter a file name. The file will automatically be saved with the required .wsp extension.

To recall the configuration and start the Floor Display:

· Click File and select one of the listed workspace names.
or
· Click File|Open Workspace
· Be sure that Look in: says C:\Digatron\Battery Manager\WKS\data
· Use the Files of type: drop down list to select (*.wsp).
· Select a file name from the list.

4.2 Care When Using a Recalled Workspace

If you use a specific workspace, remember to save it before exiting the system to preserve any
changes that you make. Just click File|Save Workspace. When leaving the system the current
workspace is stored under the name ..\data\STA-[Computer name]-UNI.wsp. If you make
changes using another workspace, these changes will not automatically be stored with this
workspace.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 55 of 363
5 Registration Data

The Registration Data environment allows the user to view data collected by the system during
the execution of a program. Viewing is allowed for both completed sessions and sessions still in
progress.

Specifically, the registration data viewer:

· Allows the user to display/analyze one or multiple sessions of collected data at a time.
· Displays data in a tabular worksheet (spreadsheet).
· Allows the user to view the data both numerically and graphically.
· Allows graphic representation of data to be manipulated by changing X- and Y-axis values.
· Allows the user to print both the numerical and graphical views of the registration data.

5.1 Using Registration Data

At the top-level menu, select Registration Data from the View menu. The registration data page
shows all session data in spreadsheet form:

When clicking an entry in the registration data, a notification appears bottom left in Battery
Manager indicating which line X of Y (total number of lines) is currently selected:

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 56 of 363
You can use toolbar Registration Data to activate some extra functions:

Refresh

Show details (see chapter 5.4)

Export (see chapter 5.5)

Find

Sort

Activate filter              Deactivate filter

You can select areas of registration data using keyboard or mouse (move the mouse with the left
mouse key pressed or click with key Ctrl pressed). These areas can then e.g. be opened in
detailed view or be exported.

Activate Filter via Toolbar

5.2 Registration Data Parameters

Each row of the spreadsheet represents one execution of a program and is considered a session
representing data collected for that particular process. The displayed values may vary depending
on the system (e.g. test or formation). The most important parameters are:
Session ID ­ ongoing number
Circuit ­ name of the circuit used
Start Time ­ time at start of the program
End Time ­ time at the end of the program. If the program is still running, no time is displayed in
this cell.
Program name ­ name of used program
Battery ­ name of used battery
Test section ­ name of the belonging test section (for BTS-600 systems)
Comment ­ here you can enter a comment for this data set

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                           Page 59 of 363
5.3 Deleting Data

Selected content from the registration data table may be deleted. You can right-click a selected
cell, column or line and choose Delete from the appearing menu. A confirmation request appears.

By clicking on Yes you will delete the sections you have selected and all associated data. Data
from programs still in progress cannot be deleted. Multiple rows can be selected to delete several
data sets at once.

5.4 Registration Data Display

You may view more detailed data by selecting single or multiple data sets. To select a single row,
double-click in the very left column of the entry you wish to view. To select multiple rows, press the
Ctrl key and hold it down while clicking one row after the other. Keep the Ctrl key pressed and
double-click the final row of your selection. For each data set a new window with detailed data
opens.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 60 of 363
The appearing window shows a table with data that is stored in the database for the respective
data set. If the program is still running, the table will not update automatically. To get an update,
click Refresh. The table initially displays all available columns of data. The registration data is
displayed in two groups of columns:

The group that appears on the left consists of the Fixed Registration Data. These columns are:

· Time stamp of the registration entry

· Step ­ The program step that was executing when the registration entry was collected.

· Status ­ The operation of the step that was executing when the registration entry was
      collected. More on symbols * and ** and # below.

· Prog Time ­ The time since the start of the program at which the registration entry was
      collected.

· Step Time ­ The time since the start of the step at which the registration entry was collected.

· Cycle ­ In programs that contain cycle (looping) instructions, the cycle number is a record of
      which iteration of the loop the registration entry was collected.

An asterisk * in column Status indicates that the circuit was in Interrupt mode at the time.
Two asterisks ** indicate a buffer overflow.

Attention: buffer overflows lead to data loss. To prevent buffer overflows you can take
several measures, depending on your system: reduce the number of registrations in the
associated program or reducing the number of running circuits.

Three dots ... in column Status indicate that the circuit was initialized at the time of the
registration.

A # in column Status indicates that the option LOM (Load Management) was active with this
circuit.

Certain devices supply data from extra channels called extension channels. Extension data
columns are initially to the right of the fixed channels. The initial display order of the column is
defined by the database. Column headings for the extension data are displayed according to
entries made in the database.

The appearance of the table can be influenced from a menu that you call by clicking the right
mouse button:

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 61 of 363
Choosing Select Column highlights the column you just clicked. You can select several columns
by repeating this procedure. Calling up the menu again and then clicking Hide Column(s) makes
the highlighted columns invisible. If you haven't marked a column and then pick Hide Column(s),
only the column you right-clicked is hidden.

All data can be made visible again with Show All Columns.

To undo the highlighting of a column, right click it and pick Deselect Column from the appearing
menu.

Use the context menu entries Number Format and Time Format to change the arrangement of
data. After clicking Number Format, the dialog Set Decimal Places appears:

Enter the number of decimal places for this column. You can set this value as a standard for all

cells of the table by clicking        . In contrast to the Time Format the

settings made here are not stored when terminating the program.

When clicking Time Format from the context menu you can choose between six display layouts:

The format HH:MM:SS (Hours:Minutes:Seconds) is the preset standard format. Instead you can
choose for a display without seconds (HH:MM) or with 1/100 of a second (HH:MM:SS:TT). The
formats Hours, Minutes and Seconds convert all times into decimal values, e.g. 2:45 turns into
2.75. You can set this format as a standard for all cells of the table by clicking

                                     . This default time format is also used for the CSV export. The Export
button allows converting selected data into CSV format. See chapter 5.5 Exporting Data for more
details.

At the top of the Print Preview window you find several buttons:

Use the Print button to go to the Windows printing dialog. The Next Page and Prev Page buttons
jump to the next or previous page (if available). Click the Two Pages button to show two pages at
once. You can then use the same button to go back to the single page view.
The Zoom In button enlarges the illustration by a step. You can do the same by clicking anywhere
on the document with the left mouse button when the mouse pointer is a looking glass . If there
is no transition into a looking glass you have reached the greatest magnification step. The Zoom
Out button works just the opposite way. You can do the same by clicking anywhere on the
document with the right mouse button when the mouse pointer is a looking glass .
Use the Close button to exit the Print Preview. In comparison, clicking the button at the top will
terminate the whole evaluation program.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 74 of 363
6 Status View

Status View presents registration data information in a tabular format. Select Status from the
View menu or click Status from the Shortcut Bar.
Status View has many features to customize the view to suit a wide variety of user needs. Features
include:
· Control
· Column Selection and Ordering
· Document Storage

Status View provides a view of the data organized by circuits and when a program is active, a view
of the data associated with the circuit. By default, data within the Status View is refreshed
permanently.
Colors for different circuit states (Charge, Pause etc.) are displayed in column Operator. An
overview of colors is given in chapter 2.10 Circuit Status and Circuit Messages.
An asterisk * behind the operator indicates that the circuit is in Interrupt mode.
Two asterisks ** indicate a buffer overflow.
Attention: buffer overflows lead to data loss. To prevent buffer overflows you can take
several measures, depending on your system: reduce the number of registrations in the
associated program or reducing the number of running circuits.
Three dots ... in column Operator indicate that the circuit is either currently initialized or (with
STO) that registration data is currently collected.
A # behind the operator indicates that the option LOM (Load Management) is active with this
circuit.

Find out more about the operation in the following chapters.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 75 of 363
6.1 Invoking Status View

The user may invoke Status View in one of the following ways:
1. Selecting Status under the View menu. The View menu is one of the main menu items. The

      View menu is also available by right clicking in the main frame.
2. Pressing the Status icon in the Shortcut Bar. Hereby it is not possible to call a new Status

      View if another Status View is already displayed.

3. Opening a Status Grid file (*.sta). Select Open from the File menu and pick the appropriate
      file. Also see 6.14 Document Storage for more details.

6.2 Controlling Circuits in Status View

As with Floor View, the User may start, stop, interrupt, or continue a circuit (or a set of circuits)
using several different methods. Common to all methods, the user may highlight whole rows of
circuits to select multiple circuits or simply click anywhere in a row to select a single circuit. When
several cells of a view are highlighted, but not full rows, then the row with the selection caret  is
the circuit selected. Likewise, when a single cell is selected, then the circuit of the row containing
that cell is selected.
As with standard Windows, pressing the control key while selecting with the mouse allows the user
to select multiple individual circuits. Holding the shift key causes all circuits between two individual
selections to be selected. In addition, the user may simply start with one circuit and move up or
down while depressing the left mouse button to select multiple circuits. The user must use the left-
most column to make multiple selections.
As with Floor View, the user can exercise control (start, stop, etc.) through the icons, the Control
menu option, and by right-clicking in either of the first two columns from the left.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 76 of 363
When selecting Start circuit(s) there appears a dialog asking for various start parameters.

           This dialog might look different depending on your hardware and software configuration.
Perform the following steps here:
Selected Circuit(s)
Choose a circuit from the list on the left.

Selection
Select Battery Type, Battery or Program / Schedule from field Selection.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 77 of 363
When activating option Battery Type you are able to make a selection in selection box Battery
Type below. The selection boxes Battery and Program then only show batteries and programs
that fit this battery type.

When selecting option Battery, the selection box Program only lists programs that have been
assigned with this battery in battery dialog. If no program is assigned to a selected battery, no
programs are shown.

When selecting option Program / Schedule selection box Program (below) shows all existing
programs.

Selection box Battery Type allows defining the type of battery. This is only possible if option
Battery Type has been chosen above. Otherwise selection box Battery Type is not active and
has no effect.

Use selection box Battery to select a battery of the type you just chose. The list under Battery
only shows batteries belonging to the selected battery type.

Use field Program / Schedule to select a program for the battery you just chose. Depending on
your system settings you can also enter either a program name or number directly and confirm with
the arrow down  key.
From the following possibilities some can be deactivated in file BM.ini (to be found by default
under C:\Digatron\Battery Manager\WKS) in section [CONFIG_CIRCSTART].

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 78 of 363
Deferred Start
When starting a circuit, a deferred start is possible.

Here you can determine date and time of a circuit that is about to be started. If you want to run a
circuit immediately, leave these fields unchanged or empty.
Comment (BM.ini entry: Comments=TRUE)
Enter a comment. It will be displayed in the registration data overview.

Order No (BM.ini entry: OrderNo=TRUE)
If you insert an order number you can use it in the naming scheme as described below. It will be
displayed in the registration data overview also.

Nominal Capacity (BM.ini entry: UseCNom=TRUE)
Enter the nominal capacity of the battery. For BTS-600 this definition can only be done in the
battery dialog, see chapter 8 Battery Maintenance.

The nominal capacity is required for using CNom (as limit or registration) and ACN or ACNX (as
nominal value, limit or registration) in programs. ACN represents a current depending on the
nominal capacity. ACNX represents a current depending on the nominal capacity and the time X
hours and is not available for all systems.
The following example shows the usage of ACN, ACNX and CNom:

Step 2: If the nominal capacity is 1 Ah, the battery is charged with 1 × 1 Ah / 5 h = 0.2 A.
           A registration occurs every 0.2 x 1 Ah / 5 h = 0.04 A.

Step 3: This step is terminated when the current goes below 1 × 1 Ah / 5 h = 0.2 A.
Step 4: This step is terminated when 80% of the nominal capacity is reached.

           A registration occurs every 0.1 x 1 Ah = 0.1 Ah.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 79 of 363
Number of Cells (BM.ini entry: UseCell=TRUE)
Enter the number of cells for the battery. The number of cells is required when using VNC (as a
nominal value or limit) in programs. For BTS-600 this definition can only be done in the battery
dialog, see chapter 8 Battery Maintenance.

The following example shows the usage of VNC:

If the battery has 6 cells, the step is terminated as soon as the voltage reaches 2.48 × 6 = 14.88 V.

Step (BM.ini entry: StartFromStep=TRUE)
Enter step number of program / formation schedule on which the circuit is to start.

Click button Start to start the selected circuit(s) or click Cancel to abort. When a circuit has started
you can recognize the new state of the circuit by its new color in the Circuit Overview.
After starting you can right-click the circuit and choose entry Show current Program from the
appearing menu to display the running program. The current program step is indicated by color.

6.2.1 Special Circuit Start Parameters

A list and description of special circuit start parameters can be found in chapter 2.5.1.

6.3 Moving Columns

The order of columns in the Status View can be changed. Select a column by clicking in the header
of the desired column. Then depress the left mouse button in the column header, hold and drag to
a new position. A red line appears on the left side of the column where the selected columns will
be inserted when the mouse button is released. The user may move consecutive selected columns
at the same time using the same technique.

6.4 Moving Rows

Select the row by clicking in the left-most column. Depress, hold, and drag to move. The selected
row will be inserted just above the red line that appears while dragging. Select multiple consecutive
rows for moving by first moving the cursor to the left-most column. Then depress the left mouse
button and increase the selection by moving either up or down. Individual cells and groups of cells
cannot be moved.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 80 of 363
6.5 Resizing Rows and Columns

Select multiple columns or rows for resizing or simply resize an individual row or column. Place the
cursor over the column or row border to the right or bottom until resize symbol appears.
Depress the left mouse button, hold and resize until the desired width is achieved, then release.
Resize multiple rows or columns to achieve the same width or height for each.

6.6 Sorting Data

In Status View, there are two methods of sorting. The first is to double-click on the header of the
column that contains the data to be sorted. The second is to right click in the column header and
choose Ascending or Descending from the menu that appears.

When the user double-clicks, if no sort is effect, then Status View first sorts the column in
ascending order. Otherwise, Status View toggles the sort order. For example, to sort the Circuit
column in descending order after first starting Status View, double-click on the Circuit column
header to sort in ascending order, and then double-click again to sort in descending order.

The user can sort based on data in multiple columns using the menu sort commands. First, select
the columns to sort. Status View evaluates selected columns from left to right. Move primary sort
columns to the left of secondary sort columns before selection to change sort order.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 81 of 363
6.7 Hiding Columns

First select an unneeded column by clicking the column header. Then right click the selected
column and either pick Hide Column(s) from the appearing menu or press the Delete key. A
warning message appears. Click OK or press ENTER to accept changes.
You may select several columns together by pressing down the Ctrl key. Always leave at least one
column, so that columns may be added.

6.8 Showing Columns

First select a column by clicking the column header. Then right click the selected column and either
pick Show Column(s) from the appearing menu or press the Insert key. A dialog appears listing
columns currently not displayed.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 82 of 363
Select columns to add. Use the Ctrl or shift key for multiple selections within the dialog. Click OK
to insert the selected columns to the left of the column currently selected in the grid.

You also have the possibility to select entry Change Frozen Columns from menu View to
determine how many columns from the left will always stay visible when scrolling horizontally.

           Should entry Configure Channel(s) not be available after right-clicking a column
