6.13 Find and Move

You may grab the vertical scroll bar by pressing left mouse button, holding and dragging. A tooltip
appears showing the circuit that the system will display in the top row when the mouse button is
released.

Likewise, when moving the horizontal scroll bar, the system displays a hint window showing the
left-most column when releasing the mouse button.

Press Ctrl+Home or click once in the upper left corner of the grid to move to the first cell in the top
row of the grid. Press Ctrl+End to move to the last cell in the bottom row.

To find a certain string, click into the column that you want to search and then click symbol Find

from toolbar Registration Data. You may as well right-click a cell or column header and select
Find from the context menu that appears. Search is always done within the selected column.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 89 of 363
A dialog appears where you can enter the text you want to find:

Click button Find Next to find the next occurrence.

You may leave the Find dialog displayed for continued use. Select a different column and click
Find Next to search for the same text in another column.

Activate Filter with Double-Click and Shift Key

In Status View you can double-click in column Program Name while pressing shift key on your
keyboard. When doing this, a Registration Data tab opens showing data only for this program.
Which filter is currently active is indicated by blue entries in registration data view. You can
remove a filter by clicking icon from the toolbar.

6.14 Document Storage

Whenever row height, column names, or column width are changed or a new sort is applied, etc.,
Status View prompts the user to save the view upon exit. If the user responds with "Yes", the
system responds with the Save As dialog. The user may choose a new file name or accept the
default and then press Save to save the customized view.

At any point, the user may click the Save button        or select Save or Save As under the File
menu to save the file.

If the saved Status View is active when closing Battery Manager, then Battery Manager reactivates
the saved view when restarting. Pressing the Status icon or opening Status View through the View
menu invokes the Default Status View.

Pressing the file open icon or selecting Open under File on the main menu brings up a file
selection dialog. Select a previously saved Battery Manager Status Grid file within the file selection
dialog to open the view.

You can change Status View's "default" by saving the current Status View using menu entry File ­
Save as... as file default.sta in folder Battery Manager\WKS\data. Now when opening Status
View using icon Status this previously stored view is displayed.

Battery Manager User Manual                       99.3           Page 90 of 363
6.15 Filters

Status View offers two ways of working with filters: either as saved filters or as one-time filters. The
one-time filter, Quick Filter, is not saved. To create a filter, right click in the grid or in a column
header and select Filter from the appearing menu. You now have several possibilities:

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 91 of 363
Quick Filter

The Quick Filter entry enables you to define a Status View filter without a provision for saving the
filter.

           The quick filters work with SQL queries that directly affect the corresponding database
           tables. Changes to the basic system configuration, for example, adding or removing
           circles, can change the filter conditions and then possibly produce incorrect results. A
           plausibility check of the results is therefore absolutely necessary after configuration
           changes.

In the dialog Create Quick Filter the field Filter SQL contains the actual SQL "WHERE" clause
that the system uses in order to filter database records. A knowledgeable user may enter the
"WHERE" SQL directly here. Click the Test SQL button to check if the SQL clause is correct:

Access the criteria design screen by pressing the Criteria Builder button. A new dialog appears.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 92 of 363
Here you can define and combine filter criteria.

Column Header ­ Click in the combo box to display a list of column headers. The editor first
displays in alphabetical order the column headers currently displayed in Status View. At the bottom
of the list, the editor displays columns that are available but not currently displayed in Status View.
These columns begin with "-". Select a column.

Operator ­ Depending on the column header chosen (see above), the editor displays a list of
possible operators. Select the operator. The operator describes how the column data is compared
to the value entered in the next section. The editor fills the list of possible operators based on the
data type of the chosen column.

The LIKE operator offers the ability to create filters containing wild cards and ranges. The user can
use the LIKE operator when working with columns containing text information such as BatteryType
and SessionName. The syntax is:

[Field Name] LIKE pattern

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 93 of 363
Examples:

Filter Type   Pattern        Accepted   Rejected                           Notes
             7%A             Examples  Examples
Multiple                               823A        % (Percent) is the multiple character
Character    72_1_           72A,                  wildcard
Wildcard                     7Batt1a,  721, 72345
Single                       7345A                 _ (Underscore) is the single character
Character                    7221A,                wildcard
Wildcard                     72A11

Character    7231[ABF]       7231A,    7231C,      Match one of the characters within the [...]
Select                       7231b,    72319       (Brackets)
             723[1-9]A       7231F
Character    7231[!B]        7231A,    7231B,      - (Dash) indicates range
Range        723[!2-5]B      7232a     7232z
Character                    7231A,    7231B       ! (Exclamation) is the exclude prefix
Exclude                      7231c
Character                    7231B,    7233B,      Ranges only work with single characters
Range                        7236b     7235b,
Exclude                                7231C

Value ­ Enter a value that the filter will compare to the data specified by the Column Header. The
unit for Value is always the base unit, e.g. V for voltage and A for current. Apart from numerical
values these Values are also possible:

· Text ­ Enter the desired text. Use the operators ">", "<", ">=" and "<=" to set ranges with
      multiple criteria.

· Boolean Data ­ Enter True or False as the value, no quotes. Use "=" as the Operator.
· Date Columns ­ Dates are tricky. Enter a complete date as follows: 9/31/2006 10:00:01 AM,

      no quotes. Or just specify the date: 9/31/2006. Use ">", ">=", "<" or "<=" operators instead of
      the "=" operator because with "=", the seconds must match as well.
· Accumulated Seconds Columns ­ These columns display "hhhh:mm:ss" within Status View. In
      the FSIII database, the data for these columns is stored as accumulated seconds. Use a
      numeric value equivalent to the combined accumulated hours (hhhh), minutes (mm), and
      seconds (ss) converted to seconds. For example, 02:15:30 equals 8,130 seconds.

To create a new criterion, select a Column Header (e.g. "Voltage"), pick an Operator (e.g. ">") and
finally define a value (e.g. "5"). With this example ("Voltage", ">", "5") the filter would only show
lines with a voltage of more than 5 V.

After defining a criterion click the button Add Criterion from Above. The criterion now appears in
the list Current Filter Criteria in the same dialog.

You can define several filter criteria separately and combine them logically. Simply select AND or
OR from the field Filter Logic. AND means the row must meet each criterion in order to display.
OR means the row must meet only one criteria to display. You can only combine all displayed
criteria together.

Battery Manager User Manual            99.3        Page 94 of 363
To delete an entry, highlight the respective line and click the button Remove Selected Criterion.
To edit a criterion, select one from the list and click the Modify Criterion Below button. Make the
changes and replace the existing entry by clicking Replace Below with Above. You may as well
append the completed criterion from the criterion editor to the Current Filter Criteria list box by
clicking Add Criterion from Above.
Apply all changes to the calling dialog with OK or abandon all changes with Cancel.
A Quick Filter is not saved permanently. When terminating Battery Manager or leaving Status View
without saving, the Quick Filter no longer exists.

Click the Apply Button to use the defined Quick Filter for the Status View. Click Cancel to
terminate the dialog and discard all changes.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 95 of 363
7 Passwords

Battery Manager includes a scalable security system to meet a wide variety of needs. In the
simplest case, the system may be configured for unlimited access as soon as the application is
started. At the other end of the spectrum, system security can be set up for multiple sets of users
on multiple networked machines. Typically, the system is factory configured to meet specific
customer requests.

Battery Manager Security

Essentially, Battery Manager security limits access to system features based on User Rights. Main
features have specific rights associated with them and unless these rights are assigned, the user
does not have access to those features. The Password Maintenance dialog provides the tools
necessary to configure and maintain User Rights.

Invoking Password Maintenance

Typically, Password Maintenance is only available for users with the necessary rights. To invoke
the Password Maintenance dialog first log in as authorized operator.

Logging In

Press F5 or choose Log In under the Control item on the main menu to invoke the login dialog.
Enter user name and corresponding password in the places provided. Press OK. With appropriate
user rights you can now choose Passwords under the Maintenance item on the main menu. The
Password Maintenance dialog appears.

Logging Out

Press F4 or choose Log Out under the Control item on the main menu to log out. Prior user
access is immediately lost.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 96 of 363
Password Maintenance Dialog

The Password Maintenance dialog provides a means to maintain user properties and user's rights.
The dialog consists of two tab controls and action items on the bottom. The tab control on the left
provides a hierarchical means of viewing members and rights for individual users, groups, and
departments. The tab control on the right shows the members or rights of the current selection on
the left.

Department, Group, User

When first invoked, the Password Maintenance dialog highlights the first department listed under
the Department tab. On the right, the dialog selects the Members tab and presents the user with
the members and non-members of the currently selected department. Click the Rights tab to see
the rights of the currently selected department. Likewise, after selecting the department, click the
Group and User tabs to see members and rights of individual groups and users. The groups and
users shown are members of the originally selected department.

Departments comprise the most general set of users. Groups are subsets of users within a
particular department. Only users can log in to the system.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 97 of 363
 7.1 Departments

In order to access Battery Manager features, the system administrator (Supervisor) must define at
least one department. The system administrator can set up a single user system with unlimited
access by creating a single department, i.e. "SingleUser", assigning all rights, and placing a check
next to Enable Rights at Startup in the department properties dialog (see chapter 7.7).

Although users may be added without a department, they have no rights and do not show until they
become members of a department. A typical small-scale system consists of a single department
with some rights enabled at startup and a single administrative user (Supervisor) with advanced
rights enabled. Regular users can then freely access some features but not all. The Supervisor
then logs in as needed to exercise advanced capability, such as Program Editor or Battery
Maintenance.

Rights set for the department are applied to all users that belong to that department. Any user
added to the department automatically receives rights set to the department. Any user that is
removed loses all rights. Removing a department right automatically clears that right for all
member users unless the user is a member of a group that has that particular right set. Set
department rights appear as an X on a yellow background.

Additional departments may be added in order to support completely independent sets of users. A
user can be a member of only one department at time. One reason for having more than one
department is to have different default startup capabilities. For example, with a large networked
system, the Supervisor may decide to set up a Formation Department and a Management
Department where at startup, everyone in the Formation department has the right to start and stop
formation but not anyone in the Management Department has that right.

 7.2 Groups

The main purpose of the group is to assign rights to individual users based on the rights of the
group. A user may be a member of many groups. The rights of the group are logically combined
such that the user has all the rights from each group. Overlap is not a problem. Rights set by the
group appear as an X on a red background.

Although groups are not needed for system access, groups may be used on larger systems where
tracking individual employees is desirable. For example, the Formation Department may have the
following groups: Shift Supervisors, Operators, and Maintenance. The department may have
the following users: Brian, Dave, Joe, Paul, and Turtle. Because Joe and Paul are Shift
Supervisors, they belong to all groups, but Operators, Brian and Turtle, belong only to the
Operator group while Dave, the maintenance person, belongs to the Operator and Maintenance
group. In this case, the rights of the group determine user rights.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 98 of 363
7.3 Rights

Rights are organized into two types:

· individual rights that grant access
· master rights that represent a set of individual rights

View and adjust rights in the tree control under the Rights tab on the right side of the Password
Maintenance dialog. Master rights are main items. Individual rights branch out from the master.
The rights shown reflect the settings of the current selection on the left.

Rights Legend

      No right. User has no access to associated function.
      User right. User has access to associated function.
      Group right. User with group right has access.
      Department right. All have access within the department.
      Some user rights
      Some group rights
      Some department rights

           Allocating rights is based on disjunction. When a user from a department or group is
           awarded with a certain right this right is active ­ even if it is not set in other entities the
           user belongs to.

 7.4 Rule of Rights

As said above, an administrator (Supervisor) may add and clear rights for departments, groups,
and users. The dialog displays only the rights of the department, when viewing departmental rights.
When viewing group rights, both departmental and group rights are displayed. When viewing the
rights of an individual user, all rights are displayed.

Double-click on a right to toggle a right. Double-click a master right to set or clear all the individual
rights of the master.

Group rights supersede departmental rights and remain set even if the underlying departmental
right is cleared. User rights are formed from the rights of the groups and the department that the
user belongs. Any rights not set by a department or group may be set individually for the user. The
system does not save user rights that are set when either a department or group has already set
those rights. Rights given to the user via the department or a group cannot be cleared for an
individual user without removing the right from the department or group.

Clearing a departmental or group right clears that right for all users belonging to the group or
department unless the right is set in another group.

Removing a user from a group does not clear user rights that were uniquely set (set when viewing
user rights). Likewise, adding a user with individual rights to a group does not lead to those
personal rights becoming group rights.
Rights Tab Buttons
Apply ­ Press the Apply button to save modified rights. If the selection (on the left side) is
changed without first applying modifications, all modifications will be lost. There is no warning. The
button is grayed (or disabled) unless there are pending modifications.
Clear ­ Press the Clear button to clear the selected right.
Clear All ­ Press Clear All to clear all rights under all master rights appropriate to current view of
rights (departmental, group, or user).
Add ­ Press Add to add the currently selected right.
Add All ­ Press Add All to set all rights.
Restore ­ Press Restore to return the rights to their original settings for the current user, group, or
department selected.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 102 of 363
7.5 Members of a Group

Click the Group tab of the tab control on the left side of the Password Maintenance dialog. Select
a group to display members and non-members of that group in the tab control on the right. Drag
and drop to add and remove users from the group or use the buttons at bottom (see description
below). Removing a user from a group causes the user to lose rights associated exclusively with
that group. Adding a user causes the user to gain rights set exclusively by that group.

Member Buttons

Remove ­ Removes selected member from current selection in the tab control on the left side of
the Password Maintenance dialog.
Remove All ­ Removes all members from the current selection in the tab control on the left.
Add ­ Adds selected non-member to current selection on the left.
Add All ­ Adds all non-members to current selection on the left.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 103 of 363
7.6 Users

Click the User tab of the tab control on the left side of the Password Maintenance dialog. Select a
user to display groups of which the user is a member and a non-member. Drag and drop to add
and remove groups to which the user belongs or use the buttons at the bottom (see description
above). Removing a group of which the user is a member causes the user to lose the rights
associated exclusively with that group. Adding a group of which the user becomes a member
causes the user to gain rights set exclusively by that group.

Tip

You may define a new password for user Supervisor by creating new user Supervisor and
allocate a password and all rights.

7.7 Enhanced Capabilities

Add New User, Group, Department

Select one of the User, Group, or Department radio buttons grouped under Add. Then click the
New button to add. If no department exists, then the radio buttons default to Department and
Group is disabled, otherwise the default is User. The New button invokes the Add New
Department, Group, or User dialogs depending on the radio button selected.

The Add New dialogs are the same as the Properties dialogs except for the title and the
department, group, or user name field. The system automatically fills the name field in the Add
New dialogs with a unique name corresponding to the type of dialog. All names must be unique
and no longer than 12 characters. Once within the dialog, click OK to add the new department,
group, or user or Cancel to exit without adding.

Properties of Last Selected

Click the Properties button located on the bottom right of the Password Maintenance dialog to
display the properties of the last selected user, group, or department from either tab control.
Alternatively, double-click the selection in either tab control to activate the Properties dialog for that
item. More information about properties follows below.

Delete Last Selected

Click the Delete button located on the bottom right of the Password Maintenance dialog to delete
the last selected user, group, or department from either tab control. A warning dialog appears.
Click OK to remove the last selection.

Deleting a department deletes the department and all groups associated with it. Users belonging to
the department remain in the system (unless individually deleted), although they lose all rights
including user set rights. These users may then be added to a new department, if desired.

Deleting a group removes the group and removes any exclusively set group rights from users who
were members.

Deleting a user simply removes the user from the system.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 105 of 363
Department Properties

The Department Properties screen shows the following adjustable properties:

Department Name ­ Enter a unique department name. Changing an existing department's name
has no effect on any department member, group, or user. Limited to 12 characters.

Description (optional) ­ Added information that appears in the second column of the Department,
Group, User tab control on the left of the Password Maintenance dialog.

Enable Rights at Startup ­ Check to enable the department rights as soon as Battery Manager
Workstation is started. The system allows one department at a time on a particular computer to be
enabled at startup. The system stores the Enable Rights at Startup setting in the registry.
Therefore, only one department at a time on a single computer may be enabled. On other
networked computers running Battery Manager Workstation connected to the same database this
must be individually set. The default setting is not checked, which means that no rights are
available until a user logs in.

Inactivity Period ­ The inactivity period is the amount of time in minutes that the system waits
before automatically logging an individual user out. In the Department Properties window, this is
the global setting that new users receive when added to the system. As long as the user remains
active, by accessing menu items, performing functions, etc., the timer is re-triggered, extending the
timeout. The default value is 10 minutes. A setting of zero allows unlimited time.

Reset Users with Individual Settings ­ Check Reset Users with Individual Settings to set the
Inactivity Period of all users to same value as the department setting, even those users with
different individual settings. Unchecked, only those users whose inactivity value is the same as the
department's value are affected when changing the department's Inactivity Period.

OK ­ Press OK to save all changes.

Cancel ­ Press Cancel to abandon all changes.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 106 of 363

under the Group tab on the left side of the Password Maintenance dialog. The description is
optional. Press OK to save changes. Press Cancel to abandon changes.

User Properties

Password (optional) ­ Enter password associated with the user. Leave blank for no password.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 107 of 363

before automatically logging an individual user out. As long as the user remains active, by

The default value is the departmental setting when adding the user. A setting of zero allows

Battery Manager provides the user with a utility called Battery Maintenance to maintain data

Invoke Battery Maintenance

or

Battery List

The Battery List is a grid reflecting the battery table within the Battery Manager databases. Each
column in the grid corresponds to a field in the Edit Battery dialog (see below). Additionally, most
columns from the Battery List can be edited directly after a double-click.
Finish each change with ENTER to save it.
To save all changes in a line, click the pen at the left:

New, Duplicate and Delete.

Edit ­ select this entry from the context menu after right-clicking a battery to invoke the Edit
Battery dialog for the currently selected battery. You can change all parameters, even the battery
name. Click OK to confirm your changes.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 109 of 363
New ­ select this entry from the context menu after right-clicking a battery or column header to
invoke the New Battery dialog (see below). By entering a value between 2 and 100 in field
Quantity you can create several batteries at once with the parameters defined in this dialog.

The suggested Name is based on the last selected battery in the battery view. If this battery name
ends on digits and Quantity is greater than 1, these digits are used as an index. BM automatically
finds the highest already used index and increments it for new battery names. Click OK to confirm
your settings.

Duplicate ­ select this entry from the context menu after right-clicking a battery. A new battery is
created that has the same parameters as the currently selected one. You can change all
parameters before confirming with OK.

Delete ­ select this entry from the context menu after right-clicking a battery to delete currently
selected batteries. The system displays a warning dialog:

Click Yes to delete batteries and associated information or No to not delete. Although the system
deletes the program assignment information, the associated programs are not deleted.

Renaming columns

The columns of the battery list can be renamed in the bm.ini file of the Battery Manager.

Procedure:
    1. Open the "bm.ini" file under "C:\Digatron\Battery Manager\WKS".
    2. In the file, navigate to "[Battery_View]" and make the following settings.
    3. Enter lines in the following format: <Col_x>=<default column name>,<new column
         name>,<changeable>,<measurement unit>.

         The column name <default column name> from "DB View vAllBatteries" can be:

              · NominalCapacity
              · Cells
              · GassingVoltage
              · MaxVoltage
              · NominalCurrent
              · ColdCrankCurrent
              · ChargeFactor
              · Impedance
              · BreakVoltage
              · NominalVoltage
              · EnergyDensity

         The new column name <new column name> can be any string. If <new column name>
         remains empty, the previous column name is retained.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 112 of 363
           The variables Col_x must be numbered strictly in ascending order and start with 1. The
           renaming oft he columns can be prevented by a semicolon in front oft he corresponding
           variable. If the entity remains empty, the previous column name is retained. All
           subsequent entries are also prevented if the numbering sequence has gaps as a result.

         <changeable>: TRUE /FALSE is used to define whether the entries of the colums should be
         editable. If this value is not set, FALSE is assumed.

         <measurement unit>: optional arbitrary text parameter that should represent the unit of the
         measured variables.

    4. Save and close the file.
               Re-opening the battery view shows the new column names and units. The column
                   names are renamed in the battery list and in the edit view. The field names in
                   Create are also renamed.

Example:
         [Battery_View]
         Col_1=ColdCrankCurrent,MinVoltage,FALSE,[MinU]
         Col_2=MaxVoltage,,TRUE,[MaxU]
         Col_3=NominalVoltage,,TRUE
         ;Col_4=ChargeFactor,,FALSE
         Col_5=NominalCurrent,,TRUE,A(INom)

    · The column name ColdCrankCurrent has been renamed to MinVoltage and given the
         unit [MinU], the entries of the column are not editable.

    · The column name MaxVoltage was not changed and given the unit [MaxU], the entries of
         the column are editable.

    · The column name NominalVoltage was not changed and not provided with a unit, the
         entries of the column are editable.

    · In ChargeFactor and the following NominalCurrent changes are not made due to the
         preceding semicolon changes are not made due to the prescending semicolon.

More functions in registration data display

Clicking a cross left from a circuit name displays additional information on the channels of each
circuit in the attribution circuit/channel. These parameters are:

The Net Circuit column identifies other circuits by the name entered as they are registered in the
circuit definition. Enter information in this column if you want to use registration channels of a
different circuit.

Column Net Channel defines which channel from another circuit should be linked to the present
one. Enter the physical name of the respective channel as it is defined in the circuit definition.

The Channel Name represents the logic name of a channel, as it is used in any dialog. All titles for
registration data columns result in entries from this column. Names entered in this column are
arbitrary (with up to 255 characters). Press ENTER to confirm your entries.

The Unit column contains the physical or logical unit of the registration channel. Physical units are
used for instance for the registration units (Current = A; Voltage = V) or for the units calculated
there from (Capacity = Ah; Energy = Wh). If several registration channels have the same physical
unit (e.g. Ah and AhDCH), select logical units which clearly identify these channels. Certain
channels, as for instance switching outputs, do not have a physical unit. In this case, the respective
channel can be defined by 0 for "off" and 1 for "on". Use clear descriptions for logical units such as
Relay_1, Valve_A, etc. The units defined in this column will be used in your programs. The
commands set in program editor columns Nominal Value, Limit and Registration operate the
individual registration channels via their units. A unit can consist of up to 20 characters.

Battery Manager User Manual              99.3                            Page 120 of 363
The precision with which registration data is recorded is defined in Circuit View. It is recommended
to adapt the Decimal places to the measurement limits. Reducing the decimals displayed on the
screen will make your display considerably clearer. The number of decimal places can be defined
for each circuit and each channel separately. For example, to reduce the decimals of a
temperature value to one, open the corresponding circuit in Circuit View and enter 1 in column
Decimal places of line Temperature.

The recorded registration values may be treated with certain mathematical functions which will act
as Filters to get the best possible display of registration values.

Selection of the appropriate filter is made for each channel in column Filter of the attribution
circuit/channel by the entry of one of the numbers between "1" and "8". The following filter
functions are available under these numbers:

1  simple average filter using 5 readings to average the value

2  simple average filter using 10 readings to average the value

3  simple average filter using 20 readings to average the value

4  simple average filter using 50 readings to average the value

5  filter for temperature probe type K                           *)

6  filter for temperature probe type T                           *)

7  filter for temperature probe type J                           *)

8  filter for special heat conductors                            *)

*): These filters will only be effective if the A/D converter in the test circuits is provided with an
     appropriate input circuitry.

The remaining columns show values that are used by the system internally, e.g. the version
number.

In an empty line you can click in column Net Circuit and by clicking the appearing arrow you
can define a circuit from which you want to use a channel. Select the circuit in column Net Circuit
first. Then click in column Net Channel and after clicking select the requested channel from the
list. Finally click in column Channel Type and after clicking you can select the channel type.
Define other parameters if necessary. A different approach is described in the following.

For circuits that are currently used, associated channels are displayed in red color. This indicates
that these channels cannot be edited at the moment.

 9.3 Copy BTS-600 user channels

User channels are displayed in yellow color in attribution circuit/channel:

After selecting menu entry Maintenance ­ Circuit Channels ­ Copy user channels a dialog
appears that allows you to copy user channels from one circuit to one or several other circuits:

First select a Source circuit from the dropdown box top left. Channels that can be copied are then
listed in the left window beneath. It's not possible to copy net channels to avoid recursions. Choose
one or several channels you want to copy from the list at the left. Do this by clicking a single entry
with the mouse or click several entries one after the other with Ctrl key pressed. Now select a
Destination circuit at the right to determine where channels are copied to. You can select several
destination circuits by clicking them with pressed Ctrl key. When finally clicking button Copy
selected channels are copied without further requirements. If there are conflicts e.g. with
duplicative channels names, a message appears giving detailed information.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 123 of 363
 9.4 Assign BTS-600 net channels

After selecting menu entry Maintenance ­ Circuit Channels ­ Assign net channels a dialog
appears that allows you to assign channels from one circuit to other circuits as net channels. Net
channels originally belong to a certain circuit but are allocated to other circuits and thus visible
there as net channels.

The dialog that appears works the same as the one in the previous chapter Copy BTS-600 user
channels:

First select a Source circuit from the dropdown box top left. Channels that can be assigned are
then listed in the left window beneath. Choose one or several channels you want to copy from the
list at the left. Do this by clicking a single entry with the mouse or click several entries one after the
other with Ctrl key pressed. Now select a Destination circuit at the right to determine where
channels are assigned to. You can select several destination circuits by clicking them with pressed
Ctrl key. When finally clicking button Assign selected channels are assigned without further
requirements. A three-digit number with underscore is added to the assigned channel in the
attribution circuit/channel. This is the source circuit number. This automatism works only once. If
the channel is assigned a second time, a conflict will occur. In case of conflicts, a message
appears giving detailed information.

           For the coherent reading of network channels:
           On both sides (circuit and logger), the channels should be indexed consecutively and in
           ascending order and be of the same channel type (NETAD or GREAL) on each side!
           If this is not the case, performance problems can occur.

 9.7 Enter EIS-Meter channels

An EIS-Meter measures impedances depending on AC frequencies. Electrochemical impedance
spectroscopy (EIS) analyzes materials whose electric charge is mainly caused by ions instead of
electrons. Examples for such materials are electrolytes and ionized gases

Circuits for EIS-Meter usage need additional channels. These channels can be added easily with
Battery Manager. Simply click Battery Manager menu entry Maintenance ­ Circuit Channels ­
Integrate EIS-Meter channels. A dialog appears:

This dialog lists all circuits that do not yet include EIS channels. Select a single circuit with a
mouse click or select several circuits by repeatedly clicking with Ctrl key pressed. After selecting
one or several circuits, click button Integrate.
For more information on analyzing EIS-Meter data please see chapter 5.7.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 131 of 363
 9.10 BTS-600 profiles

Changing values in the attribution circuit/channel can be time-consuming when making greater
changes. This is where profiles come into operation. A profile saves circuit/channel configurations
of one or several circuits in one data set. It can be restored at a later date.
With a profile it is therefore easy to switch between different configurations of the contained
circuits, but it is not a backup mechanism.

           Changes to the circle channel assignments often require changes to the subsequent
           system HW. When making changes here, always ensure that the corresponding changes
           are made to the System HW.

9.10.1 Store profiles

To create a profile, click menu entry Maintenance ­ Circuit Profiles - Store:

Another dialog appears:

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 132 of 363
You need to enter a Name for the profile. The name must be unique. On the other hand, you may
enter the same Tag for several profiles to indicate that profiles belong together. Under Comment
you may enter a descriptive text. Tag and Comment are optional.
For each input field you can enter up to 255 characters.

Under Circuit you must select at least one circuit whose configuration is stored under this profile.
Select a single circuit with a mouse click or select several circuits by repeatedly clicking with the
Ctrl key pressed.

By default, the Consider references checkbox is selected. It is located in the lower left corner.
This selection ensures that any dependencies of net channels on circles that were not selected are
automatically considered. Conflicts arise over net channels, for example of type NetAD, which are
referenced by other circles (virtual circles). If there are conflicts, another dialog opens for their
verification.

           If the Consider references checkbox is deliberately unchecked, no plausibility check is
           performed regarding the dependencies of network channels between the selected
           circuits. In this case, it might not be possible to save the selected circles or even lead to
           inconsistencies in the circle-channel assignment. For this reason, only experienced users
           should deactivate this function.

As can be seen in the example above, all involved circles to which a connection via a network
channel exists are listed. After opening, the same circles are selected that were selected in the
calling dialog. This dialog is in the background.

These circles correlate with the circles listed in the first column (net group name) of the Circle-
Channel References list view.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 133 of 363
In the fourth column (Circle name), the dependent circle to which there is a connection via a
network channel whose parameters can be seen in the other columns is listed for the selected
circles.

Clicking the Select button closes the dialog and the circles from the Circle Name column are also
selected in the opening dialog.

Since now all circles are selected between which connections exist, there can no longer be a
conflict when saving. I.e. if the Save button is pressed again, the dialog will not open again, but the
profile with the selected circles will be saved.

           If you deactivate the Consider References function and thus save a profile with
           conflicts, this inconsistency can cause problems when loading this profile later. The
           previous configuration may not be restored or may at least contain inconsistencies.

           If you delete a circle (i.e. remove it completely from the system, in the overview
           designer), all profiles that contain this circle are automatically deleted!

           A profile cannot be saved again under the same profile name. You can delete the profile
           and then save it under that name again or use a new name.

9.10.2 Restore profiles

To restore a profile, click menu entry Maintenance ­ Circuit Profiles ­ Restore:

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 134 of 363
Another dialog with an overview of existing profiles appears:

Select the profile you want to restore. When selecting a profile the circuits belonging to this profile
are displayed at the right:

By default, the Consider references checkbox is selected, just as it is when saving. The
functionality is also the same, which is why we refer here to the procedure as for saving.
In principle, this dialogue also serves to inform and verify possible conflicts. These can arise if in
the meantime further references to other circles have been configured. There are 2 types of
conflicts:

    · Channel references to circles of the selected profile.
    · Channel references (lines marked red) to circles outside the profile.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                              Page 135 of 363
After pressing the Select button, the dialog closes and the circles from the Circle Name column
are also selected in the calling dialog, just like when saving.

The yellow marked circle channel is a link that is located outside the profile, but whose source
(reference) was already stored in the profile.

The circle channel marked in red is no longer visualized and is deleted from the corresponding
circle after pressing the Restore button and the selected circles are loaded.

           In this case you must be aware that the previous configuration will be irrevocably
           overwritten and thus lost if it has not been saved as a profile before.

A profile cannot be loaded for a circle that is not included in this profile. To transfer user channels
from one circle to other circles, please proceed as described in section Copy BTS-600 user
channels.

Once selected, click the Restore button to restore the profile. All profile circles are restored as they
were configured when the profile was created.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 136 of 363
9.10.3 Delete profiles

To delete profiles, click menu entry Maintenance ­ Circuit Profiles ­ Remove:

A new dialog with an overview of existing profiles appears:

Select the profile you want to delete. After selecting a profile, click button Remove to delete it.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                              Page 137 of 363
9.11 Speed-optimized storage (only for BTS-600 circuits)

For BTS-600 circuits, there is a Speed option, which can be used to change the storage of
measurement data:

Activating the Speed option for a circuit offers the following advantages:
    - Faster storage of measurement data in the database
    - Faster display of the measured data of the circuit (especially with a large amount of data)
    - Significantly faster deletion of measured data (especially with a large amount of data)

Activating the Speed option also has two disadvantages:
    - The database may require significantly more storage space (depending on the architecture
         of the system, the set registration rate and registration formats)
    - Self-created SQL database queries from earlier Battery Manager versions do not work

           If the Speed option is deactivated, the storage of the measured data in the database runs
           as in the previous Battery Manager version.
           For channels from systems other than BTS-600, the Speed option cannot be enabled.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 138 of 363
10 Dispo List

The dispo list is only available for BTS-600 systems. The dispo list displays an overview of all
circuits that have a dispo entry with all related parameters. The dispo list can be launched by

clicking the icon from the Shortcut Bar.

The parameters have the following meaning:

The column Circuit shows the individual circuits. The following three columns contain battery
name, program name and the name of the associated test section.

In column DeferredTime you may enter a date at which you want the test to be started. The date
should be entered in format DD.MM.YY (day.month.year). The time should be entered in the 24-
hour format HH:MM:SS (hours:minutes:seconds).

The three times for Active, Start and Stop will be entered automatically by BTS-600 when the
respective values are reached.

The lines in the dispo list are displayed in three different colors to represent the three different
statuses of a test - namely inactive, activated, and started.

When you have entered a new test, it will first be inactive. The line will be displayed in black and
white. Changes are still possible. The columns Battery and Program offer selection boxes.
When you activate a test, the color of the line display will change to yellow.
When a test is started, the respective line will be displayed in red color.
When a test is completed, the display will change from red to green.
This will help you to recognize immediately which tests are currently processed on the test circuits.

After selecting a line by clicking the very left column, a right click inside the dispo list offers the
possibility to add or remove rows as well as e.g. deactivate, interrupt or continue entries.

It is also possible to start several programs on one circuit. Highlight the line that contains the circuit
you want to run another program on by clicking the very left column. Now right-click the line and
select Insert Row from the appearing menu.

A new blank line appears below the selected line. You can also achieve this by selecting a line and
then pressing key Insert.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 139 of 363
Now you can define battery and program for the new line to assign another test step to this circuit.
Click fields for Battery and Program in the new line and then click appearing button . This button
opens a dialog of the Program Manager, with which you can find individual programs more quickly
in the program management using the filter function.

Select a battery and a program from the appearing list.

You can repeat these steps for this circuit as well as other circuits.

You can also copy and paste existing lines into empty lines. First create one or more empty lines
like described above. Then highlight the lines you want to copy and press Ctrl+Insert. Now
highlight the empty lines you want to copy the values to and press Shift+Insert. Selected lines are
now copied to the new place.

Highlight the lines of the programs you wish to start by moving the mouse along the very left
column while keeping the left mouse button pressed. Alternatively, you can click individual lines
while pressing the Ctrl key. Perform a right-click and select Activate to start the programs you
selected. If there are several programs for a single circuit, they will be processed from top to
bottom.

In the following example program test1 is processed and afterwards program test2.

After highlighting one or several lines followed by a right-click you can Deactivate, Stop, Interrupt
or Continue circuits that were previously activated. The right-click menu also gives you the
possibility to Reset selected circuits.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 140 of 363
Battery Manager can switch immediately from the Status (chapter 6) or the Floor (chapter 0) to the
Dispo after the initiated start of one or more circuits. To make this possible, file BM.ini (usually
found under C:\Digatron\Battery Manager\WKS) must contain this entry in section [SYSTEM]:

Bts600UseDispo=TRUE

If you then initiate the start of circuits from the Status or the Floor (right-click and select the entry
from the context menu or F9 or via the green battery symbol), the Dispo opens with the previously
selected circles.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 141 of 363
The circles are not started by Battery Manager yet.

Right-clicking one of the circuits and selecting Activate from the pop-up menu that appears (or
pressing F9 or the green battery icon) will start the circuits. If you do not want to start all the
channels previously selected in the Status or the Floor, only mark the circles you want to start
before the final start in the Dispo.
As described above, you can also stop selected circuits in the Dispo list after a right-click. To edit a
Dispo entry after stopping, you must first deactivate it with right-click and selecting the
corresponding entry in the context menu.

To see all the circuits in the Dispo list again after you have automatically entered the Dispo via the
Status or Floor, you must close the Dispo and then reopen it.

If you try to start multiple circuits from the Dispo and one or more channels are not fully defined (for
example, because the battery or program has not been set), a warning appears that required
information is missing. At the same time, the circuits where all information is available are started.

Error numbers in the Dispo and their meaning

Number          Message                                 Meaning

    0      Registration drop                            No error.
    64         Start failed                   A registration is missing.
   249                                       Starting a program failed.
   250  Program file not found            A program could not be found.
   251         Circuit died      The communication with a circuit terminated

252     Dispo error entry != 0                       unexpectedly.
                                         Error entry is or was unequal 0.
253 Program download cancelled   The download of a program was cancelled.
                                          An unspecified error occurred.
254     Generator found errors            A program could not be found.

255 Generator program not found

The fields in the column Test Section are normally filled automatically by the system with a fixed
naming scheme.
The name is composed as follows: TS (Test Section) and a 6-digit number which is incremented
for each test (system-wide) and overflows at 999999.

It is also possible to specify a name for the individual test and enter it there individually before the
test starts.

It is also possible to specify the naming scheme to be used according to the procedure in the start
dialog (see chapter 6.2.1).

The following settings must be activated for each BM4 workstation in the BM.ini (found by default
under C:\Digatron\Battery Manager\WKS) in the respective section:
- Session naming scheme (BM.ini [CONFIG_CIRCSTART] entry: SessionNaming=TRUE)
- Use naming scheme in the disposition (BM.ini [DISPO_VIEW] entry: UseSessionNaming=TRUE)

Restart the BM4 workstation and run the program once in the start dialog with the individual
naming scheme and save. Then open the schedule and start your checks using Schedule. The
saved naming scheme is now also used in the schedule.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 151 of 363
11.1.1 Structure of the Editor using an example of a BTS-600 program

Columns
Each program consists of lines which, in turn, are organized in several columns. The number of
columns depends on the currently utilized system.

Step

The first column shows the program step number. The consecutive number is generated
automatically.
Label

The second column will usually remain blank. GOTO commands are entered here in the form of a
"label".
Operator

The third column contains the program commands that indicate which type of step should be
performed (charge, discharge, pause, etc.). If you combine several operators to one procedure,
enter the procedure name in this column.
Find out more about operators in the chapter 12.4.2.
Nominal Value

Most operators require nominal values - also called functions - such as charge (operator) with 10 A
(nominal value). Enter your control settings here. A nominal value is always defined by numerical
value and unit.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 152 of 363
Limit

The fifth column shows when the program step will be terminated, namely if the limit entered here
is reached.
Action

Enter here how BTS-600 should terminate the test step. This action will be performed as soon as
the limit entered in the same line is reached. You can control the program process by entering
different actions (next step, stop or interruption, GOTO command, load procedure). Leave this box
blank if the program should continue processing the next test step.
Registration

The last column determines when, in which intervals, and under which conditions the registration
data is recorded and stored.
Find out more about Registrations in chapter 12.4.1.

           Channel units can be used in the columns Nominal Value, Limit and Registration.
           You cannot use channel names in programs.

11.1.2 Editing Functions

Knowing how to use the editor most effectively will save you a lot of work and time. You can, for
instance, avoid entering existing program parts by using the copy functions.
Copy
You may copy program steps and insert them elsewhere. To do this, press the left mouse button
while moving the mouse pointer along the Step column and thereby highlight the lines you want to
copy. Only successive program steps can be copied together. When the steps are highlighted,
right click the area within the Step column and pick Copy from the appearing menu.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 153 of 363
You can enter copied steps either elsewhere in the same program or in another program of the
same system. To do this, go to the program and highlight the step where you want to insert the
copied steps. Then right click the program code and select Paste from the menu. The steps from
the clipboard will be inserted above the highlighted step.

Insert steps

You can add a new step to a program by right-clicking the line ahead of which you want to add the
new step. Select Insert step(s) from the context menu:

You may as well simply press the Insert key in your keyboard to add a new step ahead of the
currently selected step.
There appears a dialog where you can define the number of steps to add.

If you now want to insert steps from the Windows clipboard, right click the step above which you
want to add the steps and select Paste from the appearing menu.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 154 of 363
Edit

To change a field's value, click into this field. A button or then appears. Clicking one of these
buttons will call an input assistance.

For the Operator column there appears a list of all available operators. Please note that some
operators might not be supported by your hardware.

Select the operator by clicking an entry. Find out more about operators in the chapter 12.4.2
Operators.

The other columns might show a list of possible values depending on the kind of program, too
when clicking into the field and then clicking the button. Select a value from the list or enter a value
yourself.

Delete
You can either delete parts of a field, a complete field or one or several lines from the program
editor.

To edit an individual field, first open it by double-click. Then delete its content by actuating the
BACKSPACE or the DEL key.

If you want to delete a field completely, move the cursor      to this field (without opening

it) and actuate the SPACEBAR or DEL key. The field will be opened and its entire content will be

deleted at the same time. You can now make new entries. If you do, however, decide that you had

rather left this field unchanged, simply press the ESC key to restore the previous status. You can

also click the UNDO button to take back changes step by step.

You can also delete one or more program steps by highlighting the steps, right-clicking them and
selecting Delete step(s) from the appearing menu.

You can deactivate individual BTS-600 program steps temporarily or permanently by entering an
exclamation mark in the Label column of the program step. This step will then be displayed in
yellow color.

When removing the exclamation mark the line will be a normal program step again.

12.4 How to Create a Program

Important Note:

1. Entries in screen masks:
         All entries in the screen masks of this manual are intended as examples only. It is,
         therefore, useful and necessary to make your entries in compliance with your
         specific applications.

2. Security criteria in programs:
         The limit criteria - e.g. limits, actions, etc. - in a program should not be the only
         security measures in a system.

3. Security measures to be taken by the user:
         Damages to batteries, test units or peripherals due to a failure must be prevented by
         taking appropriate security measures. This is particularly important in the case of
         unattended test processes!

4. Responsibility of the user:
         It is in the user's sole responsibility to ensure a safe application of the program
         functions available in the BTS-600 system.

Now try to create your first program. The following explanations will help you, step by step, to make
correct and clear entries. This chapter is written in the sequence of the necessary operating steps.

12.4.1 Registration Formats

General
There is no registration format used per standard setting. i.e. only registration values for current
and voltage are recorded in the test section. In most cases this information will be a little too
sparse, so it is recommended to start the program by defining a registration format.

The registration format defines which measurement channels are to be used to register
measurement data and which are not. Furthermore, the order in which measurement data of the
physical or logical (measurement) channels is registered is defined. The registration rate can be
set in column Registration, which is described in the following.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 161 of 363
The definition of the registration format is decisive for the examination evaluation. If an
unnecessary number of channels are registered, the time required to load the test sections is
significantly increased; If there are too few channels available for an evaluation, important
information is missing. It is therefore possible to set and optimize the registration format according
to different needs.

Existing Registration Formats
Enter the SET operator in the third column Operator. The SET operator defines not only counters
but also registration formats. The format used has to be entered in the Registration column. Click
the field where you want to enter the registration and then click the appearing button .

A help dialog will open.

Here you find all registration formats included in BTS-600. Make your selection from the list
Registration formats and click OK.

Under All units all the units from all connected circuits are listed. Under Units all units from the
above selected registration format are listed.

           If there are no circuits stored in the system yet (i.e. in the connected database) there are
           no entries under All units.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 162 of 363
The following table shows preset formats, which units are registered with these formats as well as
the memory they require per registration.

         V1 (= all channels of loggers V1)              + number of channels *6

Apart from physical channels (registration data) with the units A (current), V (voltage), and C
(temperature) the system also records various logical channels. View the following table to find out
which counter is linked with which unit.

Units           Description

Ah, Wh          Total ampere-hours or total energy. With predominant discharge this value
AhStat          becomes negative.

                zero.
                Ampere-hours or energy of the current program step

AhCha, WhCha Ampere-hours or energy of all executed charge steps until now

AhDch, WhDch Ampere-hours or energy of all executed discharge steps until now

dialog above, enter a name for the format and click Apply.

Battery Manager User Manual                 99.3                            Page 163 of 363
You may select the units that are recorded with the new registration format by highlighting them in

Now all channels using one of these units will be registered. Which channels use which units can

Modify Registration Formats

To optimize registration, you can modify existing registration formats. For this purpose, select a

registration format from the dialog Registration Formats and add units with the  button or

take units away with the button.

With this registration format all channels using one of these units will be registered. Which
channels use which units can be taken from the circuit view in Battery Manager (see chapter 9).

Check whether the modifications are correct and store them by clicking the OK button.
If you decide that you would rather leave the registration format in its original status, choose the
Cancel button to return to the editor.

Please take into consideration that registration formats are used for different programs.
Modifications will, of course, affect all programs using this format. In case of doubt,
rather create a new format!

Battery Manager User Manual       99.3                                           Page 164 of 363
Change Registration Format

The SET operator at the beginning of a program defines a registration format which is valid until
you select another format by setting a new SET operator. To permit flexible reaction to existing
testing conditions, you can change the registration format at any place in the program.

           If you use sub-programs - so-called procedures - in your programs, you can, of course,
           also change the registration format there. These modifications are, however, only valid on
           the level of this sub-program. When the program returns to the main menu level, the
           registration formats defined for this level will apply!

To change the recorded channels for only one test step, change the registration format without
defining a new SET operator. Enter the name of the requested registration format under column
Registration in the corresponding line of the test step.

Please bear in mind that your modification will only apply to this test step! The next test step will be
processed with the format defined by the SET operator.

How to Activate and Deactivate the Registration Function

Activate and deactivate a registration function by using the RLevel variable. The RLevel variable
may be used to select the type of registrations that are recorded. There are two basic types of
registration records that may be written to the hard disk, i.e. user defined data registrations and
step change registrations. The data registrations include all the data the user has defined as the
registration format. The step change registrations are primarily for indicating the beginning and end
of a step. Step change registrations do not include all the user-defined data, e.g. cell voltages,
temperature, pressure, etc.

The RLevel variable may be modified at any time. The following settings are possible:

RLevel = 0.0  All registrations are deactivated.
RLevel = 1.0  Only step changes are registered, no data registrations.
RLevel = 2.0  All registrations are performed; i.e. data and step change registrations.
RLevel = 3.0  The registrations during step changes are suppressed.
              (Data registration is only performed at the end (but not at the start) of a
RLevel = 4.0  step).
              Registrations during step change are suppressed.
              (No data registration is performed at the end of a step).

To deactivate the registration, enter the SET operator in column Operator and RLevel = 0 in the
Nominal Value column.

To activate the registration, use function RLevel = 2. Afterwards the original registration format is
used again.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 165 of 363
Define registration format EIS:
After integrating EIS-Meter channels (see Enter EIS-Meter channels in chapter 9), you can define
an EIS registration in column Registration of the program editor.
Enter the SET operator in the column Operator. Click the field in column Registration where you
want to enter the registration and then click the appearing button .

A help dialog will open. To create a new registration format, click the button Create new
registration format from the dialog, enter a name for the format (e.g. EIS) and click Apply.

You can find channel EIS in the list All Channels of this dialog. Move it to the right using the
button:

Confirm with OK. You can then use registration format EIS in your programs.

12.4.2 Operators

After you have determined the registration format, continue by programming the test steps.

comprising several operators. For detailed information on procedures please see chapter 12.5.2.

Registration.

When clicking the button you activate the help dialog that lists all available operators and
procedures. Select an entry by clicking on it.

For safety reasons, we would strongly recommend you to enter a pause step of several
seconds at the beginning of a program. This will give you the opportunity of checking all
test elements and cancel faulty tests in due time.

                             circuit name;battery name;session-ID number

                             During the program run and in the measurement data

                             Subtraction operator: Subtracts the figure value of the second

12.4.4 Limits

The column Limit defines when and under what condition a test step is ended. As you can
choose between a variety of conditions, you will also have to define which actions should be
executed under what condition. This is why the Limit and Action columns are closely linked. The
action is performed as soon as the limit set in the same program line is reached. When you
select several limits for one test step, you can assign a different action to each limit.

Go to column Limit. Activate a help dialog like in the Nominal Value column by clicking button .
Now Pascal Editor opens which is described in chapter 12.2 Help Dialogs.

The simplest way to set a limit is to set a fixed time:

To meet the battery testing requirements, it should also be possible to terminate a test step
depending on actual battery values. By making the respective entries in the Limit column, you can
have all registration channels scanned. The limit is reached, when the respective value of the
channel exceeds or falls below the value set in the Limit column.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 174 of 363
The following table explains the possible entries in the Limit column.

Limit                        Example   Description
Time                          10 sec
Lower Limit                  < 10.0 K  The following action is executed when the set time is
                                       elapsed.
Upper Limit                  > 10.0 K  The following action is executed when channel K
                                       supplies a value less than 10.0. You may select both
                                       physical and logical channels such as A, V, Ah, Wh,
                                       WhDch, etc.
                                       The following action is executed when channel K
                                       supplies a value greater than 10.0. You may select
                                       either physical or logical channels such as A, V, Ah,
                                       Wh, WhDch, etc.

When entering a time, please only use integer numbers with decimals or hh:mm:ss.

Examples:    Seconds: 2.5 sec
             Minutes: 1.9 min
             Hours: 3.8 h

The entered time value refers to the program step and not to the time!
