2. Events for certain users or for all users.
3. Only show certain events (e.g. logins) or all kinds of events.

Report Programlist
This report lists all programs stored in the Battery Manager database. For every program you can
see additional data like date of creation, date of latest change and comments.

Report Programlisting
This report lists the program steps of a program you select.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 287 of 363
18 Database Archive Viewer

As of version 4.3 Battery Manager offers the possibility to view archived data.

Note with regard to changing PC's user name or password:

If you change Windows user name or password or add a new Windows user and run Battery
Manager under this name, some Digatron tasks concerning data backups might no longer
work. Additionally, Battery Manager's database might get too big. Changing PC user names
or passwords without prior consent from Digatron can lead to critical data loss !

Principle
In a first step you have to select archived data that you want to integrate into Battery Manager
Restore database. This integration process runs almost automatically.
In a second step you can view restored data with a second Battery Manager instance working as a
viewer.

Preconditions
A Battery Manager installation of version 4.3.2.30 or later with all default installation settings is
required. If changes have been made e.g. concerning installation paths, the restore process might
not run automatically.

           The entry RestoreDBPath=... in the BM.ini must be adapted to the server conditions.
           The server path of the resulting database file must be entered, no local file name.
           In the following example, drive F is an external mapped server drive:

           [Restore]
           RestoreDBPath=F:\Data\DIGATRON\MSSQL11.DIGATRON\MSSQL\DATA

Procedure
1. Integrate the archive into Battery Manager Restore database like this: click icon Battery

   Manager Archive Viewer from your Windows desktop.

   Now an archive instance of Battery Manager is opened. If no window opens in this instance
   automatically, select entry Restore Archive from menu Maintenance. A new window appears.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 288 of 363
   Click button to select the archive file (typically BMMSDE.dat) you want to restore.

   Go to the folder where the archive file resides. Select the file and click button Open.
2. Back in the original dialog click button Restore. Restoring the archive now begins. You can

   follow the current state of progress. This process may take several minutes depending on your
   computer and the amount of data.

3. After the end of the restore process you must use the second Battery Manager instance, the
   Archive Viewer. Just like in Battery Manager, use Archive Viewer to click buttons for registration
   data, circuits or programs to see details of the restored database.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 289 of 363
Note on networks:

Archived data is restored on the computer with the database that Battery Manager Archive
Viewer is connected to.

Normally this means that the restore happens on the same computer from which the
operation is initiated (local scenario). With local scenario you can either integrate local
archives or archives that are stored in a shared folder on a remote computer.
If Battery Manager is connected to the database on a remote computer (network scenario),
the archive is restored on the remote computer.

Attention with network scenario:

· Archive must be present in a folder that is shared within the network.
· Archive must be specified in dialog "Restore Archive" (above) in Archive Viewer in

      network path format (e.g. \\ServerName\\SharedName\\archive.dat).

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 290 of 363
Appendix
Options

Option: Temperature control ("Water bath control")

Temperature control: Introduction                                        System  Control Unit

If batteries get too hot during formation they can be damaged       BTS-600 -    ME              
or even destroyed. The temperature control allows controlling       BTS-550 -
the temperature of the battery or a water bath cooling the          BTS-500 -    MBC -
batteries, respectively, in order to prevent the batteries getting  MF-2000 
too hot.                                                            BAFOS -      MBT             -
The temperature control is available for the systems and
control units listed on the right (systems and control units                     TEFEU -
marked with a "").

The temperature control comprises two individual controllers:

1. Cooling water control
   The cooling water control regulates the temperature of the batteries or the water bath,
   respectively. In both variants the cooling water is adjusted in order to not exceed the maximum
   tolerated temperature of the battery or water bath, respectively.
   The details of this controller are discussed in the section "Temperature control: Cooling water
   control".

2. Current control
   The current control regulates the charge current. If a battery is getting too hot, the charge
   current of the current program step is automatically reduced. This prevents a further heating of
   the battery.
   The details of this controller are discussed in the section "Temperature control: Current
   controller".

It is possible to use either one of the controllers or both at the same time. The latter is discussed in
the section "Combining cooling water control and current control".

In the following sections first the connection and configuration of the temperature sensors are
discussed and then the two available controls.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                                Page 291 of 363
Temperature control: Temperature sensors

The temperature sensors must be positioned in the cooling water bath or at the batteries. A
combination, i.e. temperature sensors in the cooling water bath as well as at batteries is not
supported.

The values of the temperature sensors are passed to both controls (cooling water control as well
as current control; details see the following sections).

Sensors: Type:               Temperature sensor, type PT100

             Position:       One of the following (no combination possible):

                             two temperature sensors, both at/inside battery

                             two temperature sensors, both in the cooling water bath

                             n temperature sensors,  for n circuits (in each circuit at the battery)

             Interface: XI/ON (the so-called "water bath control")

Each circuit has its own cooling water control and current control. Accordingly the values of the
temperature sensors are passed to all circuits. Depending on the number of temperature sensors
(see above) the sensor values are used as follows:

1. System with two temperature sensors
    Both sensor values are passed to each circuit.
    Each controller uses the maximum of both sensor values as the actual value (the so-called
    "controlled variable"). See section "Cooling water control" and "Current control" for details.

2. System with n temperature sensors (n = number of circuits)
    Each circuit receives the value of the respective temperature sensor (the sensor assigned to
    that circuit). Each controller uses these respective sensor values as the actual value (the so-
    called "controlled variable").

Other constellations are not possible.

Temperature control: Cooling water control

The cooling water control regulates the temperature of the batteries or the cooling water bath
(depending on the position of the temperature sensors; see above) by controlling the flow of
cooling water. The output of the cooling water control, the so-called actuating value, drives the
actuator (typically a valve or a pump), thus controlling the cooling water flow.

Each circuit of the rectifier has its own cooling water control. This allows to program individual set
points for every circuit. The combination of all controller outputs to one actuating value (for the
pump or valve, respectively) is described in the sections "2P controller" and "PI controller".

Controllers  The cooling water control has two independent controllers in each circuit. They
             drive two outputs AO and D (see below for details).
             1. 2P controller  digital control value  DO (digital output)
             2. PI controller  analog control value  AO (analog output)

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                             Page 292 of 363
Actuating  The two controllers (2P controller and PI controller) have the following output
value      (the actuating value for the pump or valve):

           1. 2P controller: digital actuating value (connected to DO output)
                                  The actuating value is a digital value which becomes either 0
                                          or 1. This drives the possible actuators as follows:

           o in case of a pump: 0 = pump is turned off
                                          1 = pump is turned on

           o in case of a valve: 0 = valve is closed
                                          1 = valve is opened

           Note: The digital output (DO) is a floating output ("dry output").

           2. PI controller: analog actuating value (connected to AO output)
                                  The actuating value is an analog value in between 0 and
                                          100%. This drives the possible actuators as follows.

           o regulated pump: 0% = pump is off ... 100% = pump runs at max.

           o proportional valve: 0% = valve close ... 100% = valve completely open

           Note:             The analog output (AO) is a current output 4 ­ 20 mA.
           Note:             The max. volume flow, which will be generated at 100% (20 mA), depends on the
                             pump, valve(s), pipes, hydraulics etc. The actual maximum volume flow can be
                             configured in the program Tefeu.exe; the default value is 4 m³/h.

                             The PI controller is available only in versions ME121645 and ME121646!

           Either of the two actuating values (i.e. either of the two controllers) can be
           used. Nevertheless exactly one controller is to be used; it is impossible to send
           two different actuating values to just one actuator (a pump or a valve).

Programming the controllers (2P controller and PI controller)

           The 2P controller and the PI controller are programmed with the SET operator. The set
           points must be entered in the column Limit (see explanations and examples below).
           The columns Abort and Registration must be left blank!

           Additional settings can be programmed in the column Nominal value. These settings are
           used by the current control (see section "Current control"); the cooling water controls (2P
           controller and PI controller) do ignore all settings in the Nominal value column.

           The DO (digital output) remains active after program interruptions (BRK and INT) as well
           as after errors ER1 ... ER5.

Verification of the sensor values

In installations with two temperature sensors (see above) it is possible to verify the sensors: If the
difference of both sensor values is greater than a specified limit, it possibly indicates a sensor
failure. The DO (digital output) "delta temperature" will be set in this case. The latest program
version additionally sends a respective error message.
Verifying the two temperature sensors is done with the SET operator: The limit for the difference of
the two sensor values, followed by the unit "C", is entered in the column Limit as shown in the
following example.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                              Page 293 of 363
Step Operator                Nominal value Limit             Abort     Registration
 ...
  x SET                                            ...
                                                   10.0 C
                                                   ...

At systems with n temperature sensors it is not possible to verify the sensors, as they are typically
attached to different batteries which will have different temperatures, i.e. the sensor values are
naturally different.

2P controller (two-point controller with hysteresis)

The 2P controller regulates the cooling water pump or valve. The hysteresis is defined by the
temperature values Toff and Ton, as shown in the following figure.

                                    pump / valve
                                        control

                                    open

               closed                                               T

                             Toff                            Ton

               Cooling water control: Basic function of the 2P controller

The switch-on point Ton is programmed by writing a value "> xx.x C" in the column Limit, the switch-
off point Toff is programmed by "< yy.y C". If the actual temperature rises above Ton the pump is
turned on or the value is opened, respectively. They will remain on / open until the temperature
falls below Toff.

Programming the switch points is done via the SET operator; the switch points have to be entered
in the column Limit using the symbols ">" and "<" and the unit "C". In the following example the
switch-on point Ton is set to 80°C and the switch-off point Toff to 77°C.

Step Operator                Nominal value Limit             Abort     Registration
 ...
  x SET                                            > 80.0 C
                                                   < 77.0 C

It is possible to set only the switch-on point Ton or only the switch-off point Toff. In these cases the
hysteresis will be automatically set to 2°C. In the following example the switch-on point Ton is set to
80°C and the switch-off point Toff is not specified. The pump / value will be activated when the
temperature rises above 80°C and will be deactivated when the temperature falls below 78°C
(80°C ­ 2°C).

Step Operator                Nominal value Limit             Abort     Registration
 ...                                               > 80.0 C
  x SET

Battery Manager User Manual        99.3                                    Page 294 of 363
If both switch-points are missing, the 2P controller is deactivated!
In this case no verification of the sensor values (see above) is performed!

Each circuit has its own 2P controller, as mentioned above. Each 2P controller sends an individual
actuating value (due to different set points, different behavior of the batteries, etc.). However, the
cooling water bath has only one pump (or one value, respectively). It is thus necessary to combine
the n individual controller outputs to one actuating value. This is done by calculating the maximum
of all controller outputs: The actuating value for the pump / value is the maximum of all individual
actuating values. Using the maximum function ensures that the pump / value is always activated as
soon as a first controller sends the respective request.

It is also possible to program an unregulated (i.e. constant) cooling water flow for a given time (e.g.
for an initial filling of the cooling water bath). This is done by using the SET operator and specifying
the required time in minutes or hours. The following example shows the programming of a constant
cooling water flow for 25 minutes.

Step Operator                Nominal value Limit           Abort             Registration
 ...                                               25 min
  x SET

The different settings for the 2P controller can be programmed in one program step. The following
example shows the programming of a constant cooling water flow for 30 minutes (0.5 hours),
setting the switch-on point Ton to 81°C and the switch-off point Toff to 76°C, and programming a
maximum sensor difference of 5°C (in order to verify the temperature sensors).

Step Operator                Nominal value Limit           Abort             Registration
 ...
  x SET                                            0.5 h
                                                   > 81 C
                                                   < 76 C
                                                   5C

                             Since the switch-on point Ton is not given it is automatically set

The PI controller regulates the cooling water pump or proportional valve depending on the
temperature set point Csp (sp = set point) and the actual temperature as shown in the following
figure. The value Cmax (maximum temperature deviation) defines at which temperature deviation
the pump/valve will be completely activated (actuating value = 100%) or completely deactivated
(actuating value = 0%), respectively.

               pump / valve
                  control

               100%

                             0%                                     T

                                       Csp

                                                  Cmax Cmax

               Cooling water control: Basic function of the PI controller

Csp and Cmax are programmed by using the SET operator; the values have to be entered in the
column Limit. In the following example the set point Csp is set to 78°C and the "maximum
deviation" is set to 3°C. Thus, when reaching a temperature of 81°C the pump will be run at its
maximum or the valve will be completely opened, respectively. The pump will be completely shut
down / valve completely closed when the temperature falls to 75°C or below.

Step Operator                Nominal value Limit             Abort     Registration
 ...
  x SET                                            78.0 Csp
                                                   3.0 Cmax

Each circuit has its own PI controller, as mentioned above. Each PI controller potentially sends an
individual actuating value (due to different set points, different behavior of the batteries, etc.).
However, the cooling water bath has only one pump (or one value, respectively). It is thus
necessary to combine the n individual controller outputs to one actuating value. This is done by
calculating the maximum of all controller outputs: The actuating value for the pump / valve is the
maximum of all individual actuating values. Using the maximum function ensures that the pump /
valve is always activated as soon as a first controller sends the respective request.

Also, for the PI controller it is possible to program an unregulated (i.e. constant) cooling water flow
for a given time (e.g. for an initial filling of the cooling water bath). This is done by using the SET
operator and specifying the required time in minutes or hours and the volume flow in m³/h. The

Temperature control: Current control

The current control regulates the temperature (battery or water bath, depending on the position of
the temperature sensors) by varying the charge current: If the temperature is getting to hot the
charge current, given by the actual program step, will be reduced3.

The current control is typically used as follows:

 Systems with a water bath
    The water bath is used to cool the batteries. So typically, the batteries should stay cool. The
    current control is thus used as an "emergency control" only: Only in the case that the cooling of
    the water bath is not sufficient, i.e. the temperature is getting to hot, then the charge current will
    be reduced.
    Accordingly, the temperature threshold for the current control should be higher than the
    temperature threshold for the cooling water control. This ensures that the cooling water control
    acts first, and the current control intervenes only if the cooling by the water bath is not
    sufficient.

3 So actually the current controller is a superior controller, adjusting the set point for the controller within the
  rectifier.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                   Page 297 of 363
 Systems with no water bath cooling

    At systems with no water bath cooling the temperature of the batteries can be controller only
    with the current control. For these systems the temperature threshold should be not too high,
    since no other controllers than the current control are at place.

As with the cooling water control each circuit of the rectifier has its own current control. This allows
to program individual set points for every circuit (details see below).

Controllers  The current controller consists of an I-controller (integral control; details see
             below).
Actuating
value        The outputs (actuating values) of the current controllers are corrected current
             set points, which are sent to the rectifier.

Programming the current controllers

           The current controllers are programmed with the SET operator. The set points must be
           entered in the column Nominal value (see explanations and examples below).
           The columns Abort and Registration must be left blank!

           Additional settings can be programmed in the column Limit. These settings are used by
           the cooling water control (2P controller and PI controller; see section "Cooling water
           control") only; the current control ignores all settings in the Limit column.

Verification of the sensor values

The verification of the temperature values can be done by the cooling water control only (see
section "Cooling water control"). As described above both controls, the cooling water control as
well as the current control can be used at the same time. So the sensor verification is programmed
simply by using the column Limit as described in the section "Cooling water control".

To activate the cooling water control at least the switch-on point Ton or the switch-off point Toff have
to be set (even if no pump or valve are connected); only then the sensor verification will be
executed!

In the following example the current control is programmed in the column Nominal value (for details
see below). The cooling water control is activated by setting the switch-on point Ton to a high value
(100.0 °C) and the maximum tolerated sensor difference to 11.5 °C.

Step Operator                Nominal value Limit        Abort  Registration
 ...
  x SET                      77.0 C   > 100.0 C
                             < 75.0C  11.5 C
                             > 0.3 C  ...
                             = 0.1 C

At systems with n temperature sensors it is not possible to verify the sensors, as they are typically
attached to different batteries which will have different temperatures, i.e. the sensor values are
naturally different (see section "Cooling water control").

Battery Manager User Manual                       99.3         Page 298 of 363
Current controller

The current controller has the following three different statuses (see the following sketches too):
1. Start phase
2. Monitoring phase
3. Control phase

In the start phase the temperature is monitored only. The current set point Isp (programmed in the
respective program steps) will not be altered, i.e. the "corrected" value Icorr, which is send to the
rectifier, is identical to Isp.

As soon as the temperature rises above the programmed threshold (,,<XX.X C" in the column
Nominal value) the status changes to the monitoring phase. In this phase the current set point Isp is
corrected by the programmed factor (,,=0.X C" in the column Nominal value):

   Icorr = Isp ­ (Isp  0.X)
The changed value Icorr will be used as long as the controller is in the monitoring phase.

The monitoring phase ends if one of the following conditions becomes true:
 The temperature decreases again (see the first of the following two sketches).
 The temperature rises above the specified set point (,,XX.X C" within the program).

In the control phase the I-controller is activated. The set point of the controller is defined by
entering ,,XX.X C" in the column Nominal value, while the integral part is defined by ,,> 0.X C". High
values for the integral part will lead to a fast reacting controller. The lower the integral part is, the
slower the controller will act.

           If the integral part is too high the controller might start to oscillate or even get instable!

The current controller corrects the current set point Isp, but the correction is limited to three times
the factor ,,= 0.X C" as well as to 75% of the actual current set point Isp.

                             T

                     XX.X C

<XX.X C                                              T

                                            Isp
                                            Icorr

                                                        t

          start              monitor  control phase
         phase               phase

Current control: Basic function; temperature drop starts the control phase

Battery Manager User Manual           99.3              Page 299 of 363
                    T
          XX.X C

          <XX.X C                                                     T

                                                        Isp
                                                        Icorr

                                                                         t

                        start  monitor            control phase
                       phase   phase

          Current control: Basic function; threshold crossing starts the control phase

If the temperature drops 0.5 °C below the temperature threshold (,,<XX.X C") the control phase will
be terminated. The I-controller is then deactivated again and the controller restarts with the start
phase again (i.e. Icorr = Isp).

In the following example a temperature set point of 80 °C, a threshold of 75 °C and a correction
factor of 0.2 are programmed. The integral part of the I-controller is set to 0.4.

Step Operator                Nominal value Limit               Abort  Registration
 ...
  x SET                      80.0 C
                             < 75.0C
                             > 0.4 C
                             = 0.2 C

Summary current control

The following table summarizes the syntax and functionalities of the SET commands for the current
control.

Operator  Nom.               Explanations
SET       value
SET       XX.X C             Sets the temperature set point of the I-controller.
          <XX.X C            Sets the temperature threshold:
SET                          If the temperature exceeds this threshold the controller changes from
SET       = 0.X C            the start phase to the monitoring phase. In the monitoring phase the
                             current set point Isp is corrected by the factor given in ,,=0.X C".
          > 0.X C            IMPORTANT: "<XX.X C" must be higher than the expected battery

                                                temperature at program start!
                             Factor to reduce the current set point Isp during the monitoring phase.
                             The corrected current set point is calculated by

                               Icorr = Isp ­ (Isp  0.X)
                             Sets the integral part of the I-controller:
                             High values will lead to a fast reacting controller.
                             The lower the integral part is, the slower the controller will act.
                             The integral part must be in the range of 0.01 ­ 0.99.

Battery Manager User Manual                       99.3                   Page 300 of 363
Temperature control: Combining cooling water control and current control

As mentioned above the two controls (cooling water control and current control) can be combined,
e.g. both activated at the same time. The cooling water control is programmed in the column Limit,
while the current control is programmed in the column Nominal value.

In the following example the 2P controller is selected for the cooling water control. A constant
cooling water flow for 0.5 hours is programmed, the switch-on point Ton is set to 77 °C and the
switch-off point Toff to 74 °C. The maximum sensor difference is set to 5°C (verification of the
temperature sensors).

In addition, the current control is programmed by setting the temperature set point to 80 °C and the
temperature threshold to 78 °C. Since the threshold is only 2 °C below the set point a fairly big
reduction factor of 0.3 is programmed. The integral part of the I-controller is set to 0.4.

Step Operator                Nominal value Limit        Abort  Registration
 ...
  x SET                      80.0 C    0.5 h
                             < 78.0 C  > 77 C
                             > 0.4 C   < 74 C
                             = 0.3 C   5C

Battery Manager User Manual                       99.3         Page 301 of 363
1. Option: SOC Detection

With this option there is a special software algorithm to detect the "State of Charge (SOC)" of the
battery string during a certain pulse formation period.

           This option is only available with RailPC-ComServer and TEFEU versions ME121731,
           ME121741 and ME121742. In addition, further customization is required. If you are
           interested, please contact Digatron for details.

1 SOC by delta I

1.1 Theory of the "SOC delta I detection" algorithm
Running constant resistance discharge pulses in-between the initial charge period, the current
amplitude will continuously increase by each discharge pulse due to the increasing SOC of the
battery string. The SOC detection algorithm indicates if the increase of the actual current amplitude
compared to the previous one is less than a predefined difference e.g. 0.2 A. This condition has to
be proved for a free programmable time period of e.g. 90 min.

1.2 The "SOC delta I" in Battery Manager

Three additional parameters are necessary to set up the SOC detection. The parameters are
dIsec, dIa and dIb.

Parameter  Description
dIsec      delta I minimum in seconds
dIa        delta I of current between two pulses, function a
dIb        delta I of current between first pulse which was inside dIa value and last measured
           pulse, function b

The parameter dIsec defines the time in seconds where the increase of the current amplitude
compared to the previous one should be less than a predefined difference dIa.

The parameters dIsec and dIa are necessary to run the SOC detection.

For safety reasons parameter dIb can be used in addition to terminate the time period dIsec in
case of unexpected current increase.

Parameter dIb evaluates the difference between the actual measured discharge amplitude and the
first measured discharge amplitude when the dIa condition became true.

The following example shows how to implement pulse charge/discharge steps in a formation
schedule including the SOC detection. Please note that the formation schedule does not reflect a
real battery formation with respect to the programmed time. But it yields sufficient information to
understand the SOC detection.

The following test is made at one circuit of the MCDR 30-360-12 with 13 batteries connected.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                     Page 302 of 363
1.3 Pulse formation schedule with "SOC"

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3        Page 303 of 363
1.4 Explanation of program steps
Step 1
Minimum time for delta I detection is set to 60 seconds.
Steps 2 and 3

Standard discharge and charge.
Steps 4 to 7

Pulse charge 3 seconds with 2 seconds pause.
Steps 8 to 11

Pulse charge 2 seconds with 1 second constant resistance discharge pulse at 10 Ohm.
The cycle will be terminated by SOC detection. The cycle will be terminated when the current
difference (delta I) between two discharge pulses is less than 0.5 A and when this condition was
due for 60 seconds, or the current difference (delta I) between the first pulse within 0.5 A and any
other discharge pulse will be greater than 1.0 A.

Steps 12 to 15

10 times pulse charging with 20 seconds charge and 5 seconds discharge.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                        Page 304 of 363
Steps 16 to 18

Standard charge and discharge steps.

Numeric data display

The numeric shows the registration data of the formation. The STATWORD channel is used to
indicate several situations during the program. The value 4096 indicates that the dIa value is
reached and the timer is started. 60 seconds later the STATWORD will change to 0 and the cycle
will be terminated. If the time is not reached but the STATWORD indicates 12288 the termination
was done by dIb condition.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 310 of 363
2 SOC by delta U

2.1 Theory of the "SOC delta U detection" algorithm
SOC algorithm should be started when set voltage value (Ux) on battery terminals in pauses
between charging impulses is achieved.

Counting of delta T (dUsec) time starts only if requirement 0  U  Ux is fulfilled. If this is not
true during set period of time delta T (i.e. density of electrolyte in batteries is not stabilized), the
count of time stops and time value is to be zeroed. Time counting re-starts when requirement 0 
U  Ux is fulfilled anew.

SOC algorithm should be stopped and the formation program should jump to the following step
when time value delta T is achieved, or the requirement U < 0 is fulfilled that indicates
commencement of batteries re-charging

2.2 The "SOC delta U" in Battery Manager

Two additional parameters are necessary to set up the SOC detection. The parameters are called:
dUsec, dU.

In detail:

Parameter   Description
dUsec       delta U minimum time in seconds
dU          delta U of voltage between first value of step and last value of step

The parameter dUsec defines the time in seconds where the U of a step must be less than a
preset value (Ux).

The parameters dUsec and dU are necessary to run the SOC detection.

The following example shows how to implement pulse charge/discharge steps in a formation
schedule including the SOC detection. Please note that the formation schedule does not reflect a
real battery formation with respect to the programmed time. But it yields sufficient information to
understand the SOC detection.

The following test is made at one circuit of the MCDR 30-360-12 with 2 batteries connected.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                                  Page 311 of 363
2.3 Pulse formation schedule with "SOC deltaU"

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3               Page 312 of 363
2.4 Explanation of program steps
Step 4

Minimum time for delta U detection is set to 30 seconds.
Steps 6 to 9

Pulse charge 150 seconds with 15 seconds pause. If voltage reaches 28.0 V during pause step the
cycle is terminated and the program continues with step 11.
Steps 11 to 14

Pulse discharge 15 seconds with 15 seconds pause. The cycle will be terminated by SOC
detection.
Delta U (dU) is calculated with last step voltage minus first step voltage. If this value is less than
0.9 and this condition was due for 30 seconds (30 dUsec see step 4) the cycle will be terminated
and program will continue with step 16.
If the calculated delta U (dU) is less than 0.0 Volts the cycle will be terminated immediately and the
program will continue with step 16.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                 Page 317 of 363
Option: Load Management (LOM, not for BTS-600)

Usage

The Load Management (LOM) allows the reduction of the programmed current by a specified
percentage to reduce the power consumption during peak load times. If the actual power
consumption of the plant is close to a defined limit, LOM can be activated to reduce the power
consumption of the formation rectifiers. To recognize a critical state, the Alarm Server needs an
external signal from the power station indicating possible danger. The thereby activated Alarm
Server then acknowledges the use of LOM for current reduction.

           It is possible to process LOM values via OPC and XML with Battery Manager. For more
           information please see programming manuals OPC Interface and XML Interface from
           Digatron.

The current reduction can be applied for all program steps with the operators CHA, RCH and DCH.
Just add a program line with the operator LOM. After inserting a program step with the LOM
operator, the LOM factor entered in the belonging INI file (see below) will be valid for the nominal
current values of all subsequent program steps until the LOM operator is entered again into the
program. Thus, it is possible to include single program steps into the current reduction or to
exclude them. If, for instance, the current reduction shall only apply for program steps with the
operator CHA = "Charge", you have to set LOM operators before and after these program steps.

Example:

LOM operator to limit the current for a single program step

Alarm Server
Alarm Server observes Battery Manager servers, inputs of interface card CYPDISO 8P and Load
Management (LOM). CYPDISO 8P is a PCI card with 8 inputs and 8 relay outputs for PC.

The AlarmServer.exe needs to be running to make use of the LOM operator. The start command
for the Alarm Server must be as follows:

AlarmServer.exe /i=IniFileName

The INI file is thereby present in the same directory as the AlarmServer.exe. If its name is e.g.
AlarmServer.ini, the Target in the Properties of the shortcut to AlarmServer.exe must be
defined as

[Path\]AlarmServer.exe /i=AlarmServer.ini

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 318 of 363
Alarm Server GUI

The interface of the alarm server shows the current state of the process and gives some hints on
error situations.

The buttons allow the following actions:

Disable
Select a line and click the Disable button to terminate the alarm.

Enable
Select a line and click the Enable button to activate the alarm.

Test
Select a line and click the Test button. The corresponding output is set.

Reset
With the Reset button the output is cleared.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                                          Page 319 of 363
Contents of the INI file

The INI file that you define as part of the shortcut to AlarmServer.exe allows mainly the setting of
the LOM factor, e.g. the reduction of the current. There are some other settings that have to agree
with your system:

[Global]                 ; total number of LOM channels to set
Total_Channels=8        ; 1 = show GUI, 0 = hide GUI
ShowMode=1          ; port number where the BM3Service can connect
Port=4799

[Channel 1]         ; definitions for Channel 1

QueryType=LOM                ; kind of check (Service,Server,Input,LOM)

QueryDesc=ServiceName ; description (name) of the type, e.g. Lom123

Enabled=1           ; 1=channel is enabled, 0=channel is disabled

Period_in_Seconds=10 ; check every n sec

True_is_Energize=1           ; should always be 1

[ServiceName]

Name=BM3 Service ; name of service

InLine=0       ; input line to check

State=1        ; value for which the LOM FactorOn value is used (1 or 0)

FactorOn=77       ; LOM factor (reduced current value in %)

FactorOff=100 ; must not be changed!

Example:

[System]
Language=English

[Global]
Total_Channels=1
Port=4799

[Channel 1]                  this value is
QueryType=LOM                assigned here
QueryDesc=Lom1
Enabled=1
Period_in_Seconds=3
True_is_Energize=1

[Lom1]
Name=LOM1
InLine=0
State=1
FactorOn=77
FactorOff=100
Server=localhost:4712

Adjust the INI file to your circumstances. To change the LOM factor, modify the FactorOn value.
To reduce the current with LOM e.g. by 20%, enter FactorOn=80.

Battery Manager User Manual                        99.3                   Page 320 of 363
Option: ALOM (only for BTS-600)

The Load Management (LOM) allows the reduction of the programmed current by a specified
percentage to reduce the power consumption in a formation during peak load times. If the actual
power consumption of the plant is close to a defined limit, LOM can be activated to reduce the
power consumption of the formation rectifiers. To detect a critical state Alarm Server needs an
external trigger from the energy provider warning about a limit exceedance. Alarm Server then
allows using ALOM for current reduction.

1. Installation
Attribution Circuit/Channel
Go to Battery Manager circuit view and create channel LomVal with these parameters:

Defining LOM factor
LOM factor is defined in Alarmserver.ini as a parameter (in percent) in line
FactorOn=
as numeric value. For example, FactorOn=80 results in a current reduction to 80% of the value
without LOM. For detailed information on Alarm Server and Alarmserver.ini please see section
Alarm Server of chapter Load Management (LOM) in Battery Manager Manual.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 321 of 363
2. Usage of ALOM in BTS-600 / Battery Manager
ALOM can be used in BTS-600 programs under Battery Manager as follows.

Program Example

Step  Label Operator         Nominal Value Limit  Action Registration
1                SET                                            STANDARD
2                CHA
                             10.0 A     30 min
3                CHA         13.4 V     30 min
                             10.0 ALOM
                             13.4 V

Step 2 is a normal charge step that loads with the given current without considering ALOM. In step
3 nominal value ALOM puts LOM value into effect and current is reduced as required by LOM. In
the example the expression CHA 10.0 ALOM causes a charging not with 10 A but with 10 A
multiplied by the LOM factor. If this LOM factor (defined in Alarmserver.ini, see above) is e.g.
defined as FactorOn=80, charging is done with 80% of 10 A, thus with 8 A.

If a registration format is used that includes LOM factor, this LOM value can be recorded and used
for analysis.

Conducting the Installation

Important Notes:
1. The Digatron Battery Manager Setup DVD contains data particularly determined for

     your system. Don't use the installation DVD for other facilities or devices!
2. The installation of the Battery Manager Setup DVD requires to be logged in as

     administrator on the respective PC.
3. For the system requirements refer to the technical document "System requirements".
Terminating Applications Before Installing
Prior to installing or updating Battery Manager you have to terminate all Battery Manager
applications including BM.exe, BM4 Servers and "BM Control Service".
If a message appears that applications are still running, follow these steps:
1. Terminate Battery Manager (BM.exe).
2. Click Run... from the Start menu. Enter cmd and click OK. A DOS window appears where you

     enter net stop "bm control service". (ENTER)

Perhaps you must enter the above command from the folder C:\WINDOWS\system32. BM4
Service and servers are now terminated.

3. In case you started BM4 Servers manually you have to terminate them manually as well.
     Right-click server symbols from the right part of the task bar and select Exit from the
     appearing menu.

Now you may click Retry from the Battery Manager installation window. If all entries disappear
from the list of applications in progress, the installation start screen allows to continue the
installation process.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 323 of 363
After the installation you can start "BM Control Service" by clicking Run... from the Start menu.
Enter cmd and click OK. A DOS window appears where you enter net start "bm control
service". (ENTER)

Start of the installation process

Insert Battery Manager Setup DVD into the DVD drive and start file Battery Manager Setup.exe
from DVD's folder BMSetupDVD. The installation screen appears.

If setup cannot be started, you might have to install Microsoft .NET Framework 3.5 on your
computer first. Find the installation package on Battery Manager DVD in folder Prerequisites as
file dotnetfx35.exe.

messages regarding installations of these software modules.

Show Log to see the log file for the installation process.

Import databases from a previous instance

In the Database Assistant you can use the option Import Digatron Databases from previous

SQL Server Installation with Configuration File

On Battery Manager's installation DVD you find configuration files to install SQL Server (2016,
2017 or 2019) with the settings required by Battery Manager. These configuration files can be
found on the BM4 DVD in folder:

BMSetupDVD\Maintenance\SQLServer

There is one configuration file for each SQL Server version:

SQL Server Version           Installation file after download  Configuration file
  SQL Server 2016             SQLEXPRADV_x64_ENU.exe           bmsql2016x64.ini
  SQL Server 2017             SQLEXPRADV_x64_ENU.exe           bmsql2017x64.ini
  SQL Server 2019             SQLEXPRADV_x64_ENU.exe           bmsql2019x64.ini

You can download SQL Server Express from Microsoft. To integrate the Digatron configuration file,
the installation file must be downloaded without starting the installation immediately. Choose option
"Express Advanced" for the download if this is offered.

After downloading the installation file, start the file with the extension .exe, e.g.
SQLEXPRADV_x64_ENU.exe for SQL Server 2019, 64-bit. You may adjust the location where the
files are extracted to. Please memorize this folder.

After the installation file has been extracted, close the installation window that appears to cancel
the installation process. Copy the configuration file from Digatron that is required for this SQL
Server version from folder BMSetupDVD\Maintenance\SQLServer to the folder where the
installation file was extracted to and install SQL Server as described below.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                              Page 334 of 363
Start MS-DOS prompt from Windows (cmd.exe), go to the folder where the SQL Server installation
files are stored and enter the following text after the prompt:

setup.exe[blank]/configurationfile=[name of configuration file]

Confirm with ENTER.

Example for SQL Server 2019:

setup.exe /configurationfile=bmsql2014x64.ini

The installation will now start. Accept the license agreement and confirm several steps by clicking
button Next. No further inputs required.

           The MS-SQL Server port (1433) must be enable for remote workstation access in an
           activated Windows firewall.

         SQL Express

           All INI files are already prepared for the actual installation of an EXPRESS version:

                1. Enable RANU for SQL Server Express.
                     ENABLERANU="True"

                2. Provision of the current user as a Database Engine system administrator for
                     %SQLPRODUCTSHORTNAME% Express.
                     ADDCURRENTUSERASSQLADMIN="True"

                3. The number of Database Engine TempDB files.
                     SQLTEMPDBFILECOUNT="1"

         SQL Standard

           If you want to install the STANDARD version, you have to adjust these parameters in the
           appropriate INI file:

               1. Enable RANU for SQL Server Express.
                     ENABLERANU="False"

               2. Provision of the current user as a Database Engine system administrator for
                    %SQLPRODUCTSHORTNAME% Express.
                     ADDCURRENTUSERASSQLADMIN="False"

               3. The number of Database Engine TempDB files.
                     SQLTEMPDBFILECOUNT="8"

ComServer Assistant

Battery Manager can be used for different test and formation systems. Depending on the
connected hardware different interfaces and protocols are required. This is realized by
ComServers that are adjusted to the system. After a standard installation of Battery Manager,
appropriate ComServers need to be implemented and possibly be configured. This is described in
the following.

When Battery Manager is running you can open ComServer Assistant from menu Maintenance ­
Configuration ­ ComServer Assistant. This is only possible if no other window is open in Battery
Manager.

ComServer Assistant shows information about installed ComServers. Use button Refresh to
update the overview of installed ComServers. Deactivated ComServers (not connected to a
ServerManager) are written with gray font.

Right-clicking a ComServer in ComServer Assistant allows starting, stopping and configuring this
ComServer.

To install a new ComServer, click button Add New. A new window appears.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 336 of 363
Define under Function which type of server you want to create. You have these possibilities:
ComServer, Generator, ServerManager, OPC, UDP, FPS and AlarmServer.
You then often have to select the System that this server is used for. Depending on the selected
Function you might have several of the following entries to choose from: BAFOS, BTS-500, BTS-
550, BTS-600, MF2000, PLT.

If you select ComServer under Function and BTS-600 under System you can select the BTS-600
Generator next to the selection box ServerManager:

You can then enter a different Server name for this server. The name must be unique, i.e. there
must never be two servers with the same name.

Under Status you define whether the server is enabled or (temporarily) disabled.

Under ServerManager you define the ServerManager this server is allocated to. This is only
relevant if several ServerManagers are used. When installing a new ComServer a new
ServerManager can be implemented as well. To do this, select entry <new> under
ServerManager.

A ServerManager controls and coordinates ComServers that are allocated to him. At least one
ServerManager is automatically present in each system.

Under Port No. you can enter the port for this server. The standard port number is automatically
entered, depending on the server type.

           This must be enabled for remote workstation access in an activated Windows firewall.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 337 of 363
Under IP/Host entry localhost indicates that this server is installed locally on this computer.

Under Executable you can sometimes select one of several executable files that fits your needs
best for this server type.

You can change the Working Directory to define a different folder from which the executable is
started (if necessary).

Actions under Custom.INI and Default.INI are not always possible. You can define or view
configuration files for this server. In most cases changes should not be required.

Button View allows you to open and verify file Default.INI that defines some parameters for this
server.

Finally, click button Install to create the server with all given parameters.

To delete a ComServer, select it in the main window of ComServer Assistant and click button
Delete .

A ServerManager may be deleted with button  if there is no ComServer assigned to it.

To start required servers automatically, you need to register them in BM Control
Service. For more info please see chapter BM Control Service Assistant below.

To release Windows security queries to start the application, ComServer must be started
once manually after configuration.

BM Control Service Assistant additionally enables monitoring of database sizes and hard drive
capacities.

Manager ­ Tools ­ Digatron Service Assistant or click Battery Manager menu entry
Maintenance ­ Configuration ­ Service Assistant.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3                              Page 353 of 363
Alarm Management

When a Battery Manager database becomes too big or a hard drive is almost completely filled with
data, many problems can occur. The whole system can slow down significantly. When a hard drive
is full, Battery Manager might stop working, resulting in data loss and interruption of operation.

By using Battery Manager's Alarm Management, the size of a database and free drive space is
monitored. Battery Manager databases consist of three parts: BMEvent, BMMSDE and BMUser.
Each minor database can be monitored separately. To achieve this, BM Control Service Assistant
is used. To start BM Control Service Assistant, either click Start ­ All Programs ­ Digatron ­
Battery Manager ­ Tools ­ Digatron Service Assistant or click Battery Manager menu entry
Maintenance ­ Configuration ­ Service Assistant. For a detailed description on BM Control
Service Assistant please see the chapter above.

           BM Control Service must be running for monitoring to work.

If a monitored database or hard disk exceeds the defined limit, an alarm is set. An active alarm is
indicated at the right bottom of Battery Manager main window using red color:

Click this red field to see details on all open alarms:

Open alarms are displayed in red Status color. You can change the state of open alarms in two
ways.

Use button Acknowledge to set an alarm to mode "Acknowledged". Alarms in mode
"Acknowledged" are indicated at the right bottom of Battery Manager main window using orange
color:

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 354 of 363
Use selection box Filter to see all alarms by setting option "*":

Use button Fixed to set an alarm to mode "Fixed". When all alarms are "Fixed" there is no
indication at the right bottom of Battery Manager main window:
Fixed alarms are displayed in the main alarm window using green color, acknowledged alarms are
displayed in orange color:

After selecting an alarm with a click, you can enter a comment for this alarm:

Save a comment with button Save. Close the alarm window with button Close.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 355 of 363
After the Installation

BM4 Database Maintenance

Because registration data is permanently written into the database, the database becomes larger
and larger. Therefore, older data is automatically deleted. To prevent loss of data, each database
backup is archived before it is actually erased so you can still work with the records later.

Database maintenance therefore consists of three routines:

1. Backup: a backup is made from the current database.
2. Archive: backed databases are archived on a regular basis.
3. Delete: older data, for which formation and test are complete, is deleted from the database to

              limit its size.

To avoid deletion of data that has not yet been archived, archiving has to be done before every
erasure. For that reason standard tasks for backup, archive and delete are prepared during the
Battery Manager installation process. With Windows 7/8, these tasks are ready-for-use after that.

           For data deletion, backup and archiving it is required that the associated PC is
           running during pre-defined times (e.g. at night).

           For PCs that operate as workstations i.e. without a local database, database
           maintenance tasks (Backup, Archive and Cleanup) must be deactivated!

Note with regard to changing PC's user name or password:

If you change Windows user name or password or add a new Windows user and run Battery
Manager under this name, some Digatron tasks concerning data backups might no longer
work. Additionally, Battery Manager's database might get too big. Changing PC user names
or passwords without prior consent from Digatron can lead to critical data loss!

The following settings for Backup, Archive and Cleanup (delete) are necessary right after Battery
Manager installation and also every time you changed either user name or password or if you
want to change the times in which security measures are performed.

After Battery Manager installation you find tasks for Backup, Archive and Cleanup as follows:

Windows 7:
Click on Windows flag bottom left ­ right-click on Computer ­ Manage ­ Task Scheduler ­ Task
Scheduler Library.

Windows 8 and Windows 10:
Click on Windows flag bottom left ­ Computer Management ­ Task Scheduler ­ Task Scheduler
Library.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 356 of 363
The tasks listed here with name Digatron_... for Backup, Archive and Cleanup are executed
automatically following a certain time schedule. After successful archiving you will find three files
with extension .bak in folder C:\Digatron\Battery Manager\ArchiveBackupDB:

     · BMEvent_Date.bak
     · BMMSDE_Date.bak
     · BMUser_Date.bak

There is one archive for each database BMEvent, BMMSDE and BMUser. The file name contains
the date of archiving.

A note on database maintenance with default settings:

The databases BMMSDE, BMEvent and BMUser are saved daily (Backup) in folder
C:\Digatron\Battery Manager\ActualBackupDB.
On the last day of each month the most recent backup is archived (Archive).
Each day entries older than 31 days are deleted from database BMMSDE (Cleanup). But this is
only performed if there is an archive that is not older than 31 days.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 357 of 363
Change Setting for Automatic Archiving
The automatic procedure of backup, archiving and deleting data is scheduled to repeat in certain
time intervals. To verify and change them (if necessary) proceed like this:
Right-click an entry from Task Scheduler Library (see picture above, name begins with
Digatron_...) and then click Properties in the context menu that appears. Click tab Triggers in the
new dialog.

Here you can see the interval after which the function is called up. To change this, click button
Edit. In the new dialog you can change the time interval for this function (backup, archive or
cleanup) and finally confirm with OK.

Battery Manager User Manual  99.3  Page 358 of 363
Troubleshooting

If archiving databases does not work a reason could be that user name or password are not set
correctly. Please note above mentioned considerations at the beginning of topic "BM4 Database
Maintenance". To enter the correct user, right-click each entry with name Digatron_... and then
click Properties in the context menu that appears. Click tab General and then click button Change
User or Group.

In the dialog that appears enter the user name that is currently logged in and click button Check
Name.

Should the name not be accepted you might have to enter the domain name as well, mostly as
domain name\user name. Once the name is accepted, close the dialog with OK. Back in the
previous dialog you click OK again. Now a password dialog appears. Enter the password of the
current user and confirm with OK. Installing this task should thereby be complete

In case of problems follow this procedure for every task (Backup Archive, Cleanup) that might not
work correctly.
